# AstroFlows mosaics: the TARGET block and the panel loop

- Date: 2026-09-23
- Status: design, being built. - Scope: AstroFlows (server `server/astrodeck/flows/`, the sequence engine, both flows UIs), plus the framing and rotation seams a mosaic touches.

## Owner's brief (verbatim)

> the best way to implement mosaics in astroflows. I think this means we need the concept of a target block that replaces the slew and solve. Inside of this target modal we should have a screen that shows us a sky survey and allows us to set position, rotation, mosaic alignment, etc. This should then be able to feed into a filter cycle block. The two should feed in a circle until the designated quantity of subs have been captured for all mosaic panels before moving on to whatever steps are next. Keep an eye out for any missing features that should be implemented via ultracode or when it would be most appropriate to file an issue for those to follow up later. Be bold.

## How this spec was made

Three designs competed: engine-first (A), operator-first (B) and campaign-first (C). Three judges scored them on safety and correctness, operator experience, and implementability. This spec starts from A, which won on two of the three lenses. It adds every item the judges marked "must keep" from B and C, and it fixes every fatal flaw the judges found. Appendix B lists each fix.

Every number here was computed with the repo's own `framing.compute_mosaic` and `framing.project` (`server/astrodeck/catalog/framing.py:100-231`). Appendix A gives the formulas, so each number can be recomputed.

Site privacy: nothing in this document names the site. Per-panel altitude, setting times, hour angles and meridian times are site-derived, and they are treated that way throughout (section 6.9).

---

## 0. The design on one page

A mosaic is **one TARGET block**. The existing TARGET node grows into it. There is no new node type. The block absorbs SLEW + CENTER, whose settings now reach the run. It stores a framing spec as flat params: centre, angle mode and PA, rows x cols, overlap, a snapshot of the camera field, and skipped panels. It also stores a visiting policy.

A modal opens from the block. It shows the sky survey with the grid laid over it. There the operator sets position, angle, grid and overlap, and toggles panels.

The block feeds a FILTER CYCLE. The circle the owner asked for is **drawn** as a dashed amber event wire from the "pass done" output of the **last stage of the panel lane** (usually the FILTER CYCLE) back to TARGET "next panel". The panel lane is the unbroken chain of AUTOFOCUS, GUIDE, CAPTURE LOOP and FILTER CYCLE nodes after the TARGET (section 1.5). The editor adds that wire automatically when a block becomes a mosaic. The wire is the single source of truth for "rotate panels every pass". Without it, each panel runs to completion. The last stage's "all done" fires when every panel has every stage's quota of accepted subs. The cursor then moves on to whatever that output is wired to.

At compile time `to_plan` expands the block into N panel Targets through `compute_mosaic`, the same function the modal previews. Panel ids are deterministic and keyed to the geometry. The plan gains one `TargetGroup`.

The engine rotates between panels by list surgery inside the existing scheduler loop, with no second scheduler. Each visit runs through the unchanged per-frame gate stack. The rules that govern the rotation:

- Blocked panels read "waiting" where gating is decided.
- While nothing can be shot, the mount is park-held on the idle clock (how long nothing has been shootable), never on the length of one wait interval.
- The mount changes pier side at most once per group per night.
- Failures set a panel aside for tonight, never mark it done. A panel that rejects while the others accept is set aside after three such visits.
- A panel whose guider will not start goes to the back of the rotation and is retried on the next pass, whatever the rig's `guiding_action`, and is set aside after three such passes (Revision 2, ruling 5).
- A pass that takes no exposures ends the rotation.
- While the whole mosaic waits, the scheduler shoots later targets and comes back (the owner's default, a per-flow option). Their visits end when the next panel is due, so they cannot take the rest of the night.

Pressing Run on night two continues the flow's own dormant session, so there is one ledger per flow and `Session.owed()` stays the only definition of finished.

### Decisions

| # | Decision | Why, in one line |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Extend the TARGET node (type id stays `target`). Do not add a node type. | Saved flows keep loading. One node expands to up to 100 panels, far inside the 400-node cap (`flows/models.py:101-102`). The palette union is unchanged. |
| D2 | The circle is a drawn **structural event wire**, `<last stage>.pass -> target.next`, where the last stage is the tail of the panel lane (1.5). It is never a flow back-edge and never a new port kind. | Flow back-edges are silently dropped today (`compile.py:72-78`). A third port kind breaks the kind-to-kind rule and `flow_order` (`flows/models.py:143`, `compile.py:77-78`). Backward event wires are already legal (`design_handoff_astrodeck_flows/README.md:122`). |
| D3 | The default is **pass-major**: rotate panels every pass, one full filter pass per visit, least complete first. Panel-first is what you get by deleting the wire. Filter-major is rejected. | It extends FILTER CYCLE's own reason for existing ("a night cut short still stacks") from channels to panels. A local, untracked mobile handoff draft describes the same rotation (`ASTRODECK-UPDATE/design_handoff_astrodeck_mobile/README.md:174`); it is not a committed contract, and it numbers panels row-major at 15% overlap, which this spec does not follow. |
| D4 | Panels are derived, never stored. Compile emits one entry per block and `to_plan` expands it. | The graph is the source of truth (`flows/models.py:3-8`). The server is canonical for slew targets (`framing.py:3-8`). |
| D5 | Identity is deterministic (uuid5) and keyed to the block's **anchor**: the geometry at which its counts started. Skipping a panel is not part of the identity. A re-frame that moves every panel corner less than half the overlap width (`REFRAME_CARRY_FRACTION = 0.5`, a first guess) keeps the anchor and carries the counts; a larger one re-anchors and restarts them, and the modal says so before DONE (Revision 2, ruling 3). | Counts stay honest after a real re-frame, a nudge does not throw away a campaign, and continuation works across nights. Measuring against the anchor, never the last save, stops small nudges adding up. This still fixes C's flaw of crediting old frames to moved panels. |
| D6 | One ledger per flow. Run CONTINUES the flow's dormant session through the existing id-safe plan edit plus `engine.start(session=)`. | No second ledger and no session schema change. `owed()` stays single (`session.py:143-152`). |
| D7 | Engine: `SequencePlan.groups`. Rotation is a requeue inside `_run_scheduled`. Visits are bounded by passes in `_run_steps`. The `_run_step` gate stack is untouched. | There is one scheduler to keep correct. Per-panel gating, waits, park-hold, skips and jumps come for free. |
| D8 | Every new TARGET block, single target or mosaic, counts **accepted** subs only (owner ruling, 2026-09-24). The editor no longer offers "every sub taken". A flow saved before this change keeps counting attempts when it loads, shows a one-line notice, and switches to accepted subs when it is next saved (Revision 2, ruling 2). | Rejected frames must not fill a quota. Loading never changes a flow's meaning silently; the save that changes it is announced first. |
| D9 | Reachability is decided in the scheduler's selection, so a blocked panel is "waiting" and the existing do-while wait path runs. A verdict that waiting cannot change (no site, a pier change with flips off) is a refusal, never a wait. The mount is park-held on the idle clock. | This removes the hot loop the safety judge found in A and C, and the tracking-while-idle hole the critic found (5.1). |
| D10 | Meridian: pier hysteresis on the hour angle with the **plan's** flip lead as the margin (never the lead learned by #127), verified by reading the pier side after every hop. A visit ends before its panel's flip point. The flip-owed memory is kept per target. | At most one pier change per group per night, the #136 invariant stays armed, and the pre-flip idle is priced and bounded (5.7). |
| D11 | Angle has three explicit modes: any, rotate to, camera fixed. A mosaic requires an angle. Every hop compares the angle its centring solve measured with the planned angle, whatever the rotator state. | A dropped rotator skips rotation silently today (`hub.py:6455-6456`), and a fixed camera at the wrong angle cannot tile. |
| D12 | Failures set a panel aside for tonight. Deferrals are bounded. The group anti-spin guard counts exposures, not accepted frames. | "Set aside" is not "done", and a clouded pass must not end a mosaic. |
| D13 | One shared modal module for both UIs, opened from the per-type slot. DONE is one atomic write, made after the server has answered with panel centres. | This avoids a fork between the classic and #/next UIs, and prevents a half-applied framing. |
| D14 | Per-panel PA correction for meridian convergence waits for #145. Until then a doctor rule states how much overlap convergence costs. | The sign of the correction cannot be validated on the simulator (`solve/simsolver.py:97`). |
| D15 | "What comes next" is whatever the tail's "all done" output is wired to. By default ("Shoot later targets, then come back"), while every live panel is waiting, the scheduler shoots a later target in visits that end when the next panel is due; once the group is set aside tonight, a later target runs its normal course. The default is the owner's ruling of 2026-09-24 ("No sense in wasting time due to an obstruction"). It is a **per-flow option**, `FlowGraph.settings.whenWaiting`, and "Wait for the mosaic" is its other value (Revision 2, ruling 1). | This honours the owner's "before moving on" without idling a clear sky, and without handing the night away at every routine wait (1.6). |

### Earlier decisions this reverses (named, not silently overturned)

1. `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-14-mosaic-apply-steps-design.md:32-37` kept the Atlas geometry-only, with no live group step editor and no server awareness of group steps. A mosaic block that feeds one FILTER CYCLE is exactly a live group step list, and the server now knows about groups.
2. `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-13-plan-schedule-design.md:98-106` declined a rows/cols round trip. The block now stores rows/cols and reopens the framing it was made from.
3. The Flows handoff left "Mosaic panels as a node or a Target detail?" open (`design_handoff_astrodeck_flows/AstroDeck Flows.dc.html:565`). This spec rules that a mosaic is a Target detail, and that the loop is a backward event wire the compile consumes as structure. Record the ruling as a README amendment next to `README.md:122` and `:163`, so later agents do not "fix" it back. Mark `MILESTONE2-CONTRACT.md` superseded for the node count (19 there, 21 shipped). Both are drafted as uncommitted edits labelled "Amendment 2026-09-24 (owner approval pending)", and wait for the owner's signature (Revision 2, ruling 6).
4. The Atlas and Sky "Send N panels to Plan" door is retired and replaced by "Send to Flow Wizard" (Revision 2, ruling 4, #196). With it goes S2b, the classic Plan "cycle panels each pass" toggle.

---

## 1. Block model and wiring

### 1.1 The lane

```
DUSK WINDOW --window--> TARGET (M31, 3x2) --each panel--> AUTOFOCUS --> GUIDE --> FILTER CYCLE --all done--> SESSION REPORT
                           ^  next panel (event)                                        |
                           +------------ pass done (event, dashed amber back-arc) -------+
FILTER CYCLE --frame graded (event)--> CONDITION / NOTIFY        (unchanged)
```

- The flow lane stays acyclic. `flow_order` classifies edges by the source port's kind (`compile.py:77-78`), so an event edge never enters Kahn's walk.
- AUTOFOCUS and GUIDE sit inside the drawn circle because their effects are re-established on every panel hop (section 5.6). Their params stay presence-only, as today (`to_plan.py:361-385`).
- Every node type has at most one flow input (checked across all 21 in `flows/nodes.py`), so a lane is a chain and stages follow each other only through a stage's `complete` output. Section 1.5 turns that into the ownership rule.
- "Moving on to whatever steps are next" is the flow wire out of the tail stage's "all done". It fires once, when every non-skipped panel owes nothing (section 1.6).

### 1.2 TARGET block

The type id stays `target`, category SOURCE, label TARGET.

| Port | Kind | Required | Label | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `arm` | flow in | yes | arm | unchanged |
| `next` | event in | no (added to `optional_ins`, `nodes.py:84`) | next panel | NEW; only a `pass` wire from the tail of this block's panel lane means anything here |
| `target` | flow out | - | each panel | id unchanged, so every saved wire survives; label changes |

- Params are listed in section 3.1.
- With rows = cols = 1 the block is today's single target, plus centring settings that now reach the engine.
- Card footer `sum()`, sized to the 188 px card:
  - Mosaic rotating: `M31 . 3x2 . PA 30.0 . 25% . rotate`
  - Mosaic without the wire: `M31 . 3x2 . one panel at a time`
  - Single target: `NGC 7331 . any angle`
- A one-line state chip from the progress route S1 built, `GET /api/flows/{flow_id}/progress` (`CAP_VIEW_STATUS`, S1 item 9). Its answer is `flow_progress`'s in `flows/progress.py`: one entry in `blocks` per TARGET node (one per POOL node, whose members are its panels), each with `banked`, `owed` and `total` and one entry in `panels` per panel, counted from the flow's session as Run reads it (5.9), plus the frames orphaned on steps the flow no longer has. The chip reads `212/315 subs` for a single target (the block's `banked` over its `total`), which is the chip S1 draws, on both canvases (`progressChip`); for a mosaic it will read `4/6 panels done` (the panels whose `owed` is 0), which S3 adds.

**As built** (S3, #189). The route answers a mosaic. A multi-panel TARGET's block holds one entry in `panels` per panel its group shoots, in the plan's order and each at its own 0-based `row` and `col`, found through the group id `to_plan` mints (`identity.group_id` over `_block_key`, the block's anchor first), and it adds two keys no other block carries: `grid` (`rows`, `cols`) and `skipped`, each panel the skip list names (`TargetGroup.skipped_ids`) with what the ledger holds on it, counted in the session's count mode and uncapped, in none of the block's sums and not counted as orphaned. A block whose every panel is skipped has no group, so it has no panels, and its skips are the panels the plan's own `skipped_ids` names (S4, #335), the set CONTINUE reads (5.9); until S4 they came from the compile's `skip`, which CONTINUE could not see, so the card showed that block's frames held while CONTINUE refused them as dropped. A single target is found on the key `to_plan` gives a 1x1 block, its anchor and the angle it is laid out at (`_single`), so a block at "Camera fixed at PA", or one nudged since its anchor, no longer fails the route. The chip is drawn (S3, #189): `progressChip` (`ui/src/components/flows/flowProgress.ts`), the one formatter both canvases draw, reads a TARGET block whose `grid` holds more than one panel as `N/M panels done` (`panelsChip`), N the panels whose `owed` is 0 and M the panels in `panels`, so a skipped panel is in neither; it draws no chip when no panel is shot or a panel's `owed` is not a finite number, and a single target still reads `212/315 subs`.

**As built** (S4, #189). The card footer is `targetFooter` in `ui/src/components/flows/targetSummary.ts`, drawn by both canvases (`FlowNodeCard.tsx` and next's `FlowNode.tsx`); `targetSummary(node, graph)` is the same line computed from the graph alone. Whether the block rotates is read from the loop wire, never from a param (`targetLoops`, over the editor's `loopWires`), so the card cannot say "rotate" over a graph the run shoots panel-first. S4 wrote the three lines as the design has them, with the separator every card footer already uses: `M31 · 3x2 · PA 30.0 · 25% · rotate`, `M31 · 3x2 · one panel at a time` and `NGC 7331 · any angle`. The grid is written columns by rows (S4 orchestrator ruling 1), a fixed camera reads `fixed PA 30.0`, the name is the TARGET's own `sum`, called once per render, and a block with no name yet leaves the name out rather than open the line with a separator. "Sized to the 188 px card" does not hold on the classic card: about 29 characters of its 9.5 px mono fit, the rotating line is 34, and `truncate` cut it inside "rotate" (#357).

**As built since S5** (S5, #357). The loop word comes second, after the name, so where the classic card's `truncate` cuts the line it cuts the grid and the angle first and keeps the word that says how the panels are shot: `M31 · rotate · 3x2 · PA 30.0 · 25%` and `M31 · one panel at a time · 3x2`, and a single target reads `NGC 7331 · any angle` as before. The classic footer's budget is 29 characters, computed from the card's width, border, padding and type (`FlowNodeCard.tsx`, `cardFooterDom.test.tsx`), and until S7 a line past it showed one character fewer, the last taken by the ellipsis: "rotate" stayed whole behind a name of up to 19 characters, and "one panel at a time" was cut behind a name longer than 6, `NGC 7331 · one panel at a ti`, which asked for a shorter word (noted on #357); S7 fits the line instead (below). The card says "rotate" only while no pass wire enters the block from mid-lane beside its loop wire (`targetLoops`, #410; 1.4).

**As built since S7** (S7, #357, S7 orchestrator ruling 9). The classic card no longer leaves its footer to `truncate`: it draws `fittedFooter`, the `targetFooter` line fitted to `CLASSIC_FOOTER_CHARS`, the 29 characters `FlowNodeCard.tsx` computes from the card's width, border, padding and type (`monoChars`), which `cardFooterDom.test.tsx` holds to the mounted card. A line that does not fit drops the overlap first, then shortens the name with an ellipsis (`FOOTER_ELLIPSIS`), keeping at least one of its characters beside the grid; when not even one fits there, the grid goes next and the name is fitted again, keeping at least one of its characters beside the loop word and the angle words; the name is left out only when none of its characters fits even then. The loop word and the angle words, the mode and the PA, are never cut. The rotating 2x2 whose PA the #357 probe saw cut reads `M31 · rotate · 2x2 · PA 55.0`; the panel-first line, which leaves the name only one character of room (29 less the 28 of ` · one panel at a time · 3x2`), reads `M31 · one panel at a time` and `NGC 73… · one panel at a time` once the grid gives way before the name's last character (noted on #357, for the owner). The whole line is the footer's tooltip whenever anything was left out, and #/next keeps the whole line and wraps it.

### 1.3 FILTER CYCLE and CAPTURE LOOP

1. Both gain an event output `pass`, labelled "pass done". It is structural: it means something only when it leaves the tail of a panel lane and enters the owning TARGET's `next`.
   - `_trigger_for` (`compile.py:124-164`) gets an explicit branch for `from_port == "pass"` that returns `"<type>.pass"`. Today capture and cycle return `on_frame_graded` for any port (`compile.py:149-150`).
   - A pass wire to any other destination is therefore an illegal trigger, and `to_plan` reports it as "this rule will not run" at warn level through the existing machinery (`to_plan.py:596-728`).
2. FILTER CYCLE's and CAPTURE LOOP's `complete` output is relabelled "all done". Port labels are per type, so the label is the same on every card. Only the tail's "all done" is "what runs next". A mid-lane stage's `complete` wire only says "the next stage belongs to the same panel", and the wire's label chip reads "then".
3. Under a multi-panel block, every panel owes `cycles x perCycle` subs of every ticked slot. That is the same formula as a single target today (`to_plan.py:515-558`), so each panel is its own quota. One **pass** is one round of `perCycle` subs for every slot still short on that panel, in wheel order.
4. A CAPTURE LOOP inside the circle is treated as a one-slot cycle: acquisition `cycle`, `per_visit` 1, count per panel. Mono operators need no one-slot FILTER CYCLE workaround.
5. Several stages in one panel lane are concatenated in lane order. For example, TARGET -> CYCLE LRGB -> CAPTURE Ha 300 gives every panel all eight steps, the CAPTURE is the tail, and the loop wire leaves the CAPTURE. One pass is one round of `per_visit` frames for every step still short on that panel, in lane order and then wheel order. Any cycle step, or a loop wire, marks every panel `acquisition = "cycle"`.

**As built** (S3, #189). Both types carry the `pass` event output, labelled "pass done", and a `complete` output labelled "all done" (`nodes.py`), and TARGET the optional `next` input, labelled "next panel". `_trigger_for` answers `capture.pass` or `cycle.pass` for a wire out of `pass`, a trigger the engine does not have, so item 1's machinery reports a pass wire that is not a loop wire, at warn level; the loop wire itself is never emitted (1.4). Inside the circle a CAPTURE LOOP's step carries `per_visit` 1, and `to_plan` marks every panel `acquisition = "cycle"` when the block loops or any stage it owns cycles (`_expand_mosaic`).

**As built** (S4, #331). AUTOFOCUS and GUIDE carry the `pass` output too, labelled "pass done" and listed after `focused` and `guiding`, so that each can be the stage the loop wire leaves: a lane may end on any lane node (1.5), and until S4 one that ended on an AUTOFOCUS or GUIDE had no loop wire to give. Every lane type but legacy SLEW now has the port, and the compile reads which types have it off the vocabulary (`PASS_TYPES`), so `_trigger_for` asks its `pass` branch first and answers `autofocus.pass` or `guide.pass` as it answers `capture.pass` or `cycle.pass`, a trigger the engine does not have, reported at warn level for a pass wire that is not a loop wire, and a pass wire into a one-panel TARGET from any of the four is consumed as 1.4 says. Items 3 to 5 are unchanged: an AUTOFOCUS or GUIDE adds no step, so a pass is still a round of the capture and cycle steps.

### 1.4 The circle: the loop wire

**Grammar**

1. `stage.pass -> target.next` is legal when the stage is the **tail** of that TARGET's panel lane (section 1.5) and the block is multi-panel. It compiles to `group.mode = "rotate"`. Without such a wire, a multi-panel block compiles to `mode = "sequential"`, which is panel-first. Deleting the wire changes only the mode, never which stages the panels own.
2. An event input takes many wires (`flows/models.py:149-167`). More than one pass wire into `next` gets a doctor note ("one is enough") and changes nothing.
3. `to_plan` consumes the wire as structure and never emits it as an Instruction. The precedent is the calib wires that are honoured by `plan_extras` (`HONOURED_BY`, `to_plan.py:156-160`).
4. A pass wire into a 1x1 block gets a note: "one panel, nothing to rotate between." A pass wire from a stage in another block's lane gets a warning: "this wire does nothing". A pass wire from a stage of this lane that is not the tail is M12, a danger, because the stages after it would be shot once per panel with nothing to say when.
5. **Flow back-edges become a structural error** (slice S0).
   - `FlowGraph.validation_errors` refuses them with: "this flow loops back on itself at FILTER CYCLE -> TARGET; a flow lane runs once, and the panel loop is the dashed 'pass done' wire".
   - Save refuses with 422, and so does `/run`.
   - Both editors' drop resolvers refuse the wire with the same sentence (`ui/src/components/flows/FlowCanvas.tsx:107-139`, next `canvasModel.ts:155-186`).
   - The false docstring at `compile.py:72-75` ("a flow cycle is not expressible in the editor") is corrected.

**When the wire is added.** It is added automatically only at these moments, and it always leaves the tail of the panel lane (1.5). It is never added as a side effect of connecting something else.

- Modal DONE, when the block becomes multi-panel and owns a stage.
- The wizard's mosaic kind, including a mosaic sent from the Sky or the Atlas through "Send to Flow Wizard" (slice S6, #196).
- The one-tap LOOP PANELS button on the card, in the stage list, and as the RUN section toggle in the modal.

**When it is deleted.** The block runs panel-first. Doctor M3 warns. The card reads "one panel at a time". The stage-list rail disappears.

**How it is drawn** (slice S4).

- `FlowWireLayer` draws any event edge whose destination is `target.next` as a back-arc. The path leaves the source port, drops to `y = max(bottom of the owned body cards) + 28 px`, runs left, and rises into the `next` port.
- The stock S-bezier (`ui/src/components/flows/geometry.ts:199-212`) would sweep back across the body cards. The arc is computed from the card formula (`geometry.ts:65-74`) and is never measured from the DOM.
- It carries a label chip: `every pass: next panel . 6 panels`.
- In night mode it is told apart by the dash pattern and the label, not by hue.
- The phone stage list has no canvas (`ui/src/next/hubs/session/flows/canvas/FlowStagesPhoneSheet.tsx:1-50`). There the loop is a dashed amber rail from the TARGET row to the FILTER CYCLE row, the rows inside it are indented, and the rail is labelled `EVERY PASS: NEXT PANEL`. When the wire is absent, a `LOOP PANELS` button takes its place.

**Why a wire rather than a param.**

- The owner asked to see the circle.
- The phone operator's only view of the graph is wires as rows.
- The grammar already allows backward event wires, with REPORT "done" -> POOL "advance" as precedent (`README.md:122`).

The implementability judge accepted exactly this form. The risk that the wire is lost silently is closed by the S0 `flowsConnect` fix (section 9, item I-06) and by doctor M3.

**As built** (S3, #189). The grammar is built in the compile and `to_plan`. A pass wire from the tail of a multi-panel block's lane into that block's `next` is its loop wire (`loop_wires`), and so is every copy of it, so two loop wires are one loop, and the doctor's M4 notes that one is enough. The compile consumes it as the entry's `loop` and emits no rule for it (`_loop_edge_keys`), and `to_plan` gives the block's group `mode = "rotate"`, or `"sequential"` without one. A pass wire into a 1x1 block is no loop wire: the doctor's M4 notes that one panel has nothing to rotate between, as item 4 says. S3 emitted it as it emits any other pass wire, and `to_plan` reported it as a rule that would not run, at warn level, so `/run` asked for it to be accepted where item 4 designed only a note (#349); S4 made it a note everywhere (below). The editor never adds the wire when something else is connected, and it moves it: the connect that appends a stage after the tail carries the lane's loop wire to the new tail in the same write (1.5 item 6).

**As built** (S4, #349, S4 orchestrator ruling 3). A pass wire into a one-panel TARGET's `next` from a stage of that TARGET's own lane is structure, as the loop wire is: `one_panel_pass_wires` lists it, and the compile consumes it and emits no rule for it, so `to_plan` reports no loss and `/run` does not ask. It makes nothing a one-slot cycle, since one panel has no pass to rotate, and it is consumed from any stage of the lane, the tail or not: with one panel there is no later panel for the stages after the wire's source to be skipped on, so M12's reason does not arise. The doctor's M4 notes exactly the wires in that list, so the note and the consumption are one set. Every other pass wire that is not a loop wire is still emitted and reported as a rule that will not run: one from a stage of another block's lane (M4's warning, a stage past a DOME included, which `owner_of` gives to nobody), one into any port but a TARGET's `next`, and one from mid-lane into a multi-panel block (M12).

**As built, how it is drawn** (S4, #189). `geometry.loopArc` routes the wire from the card formula (`cardBox`, over `nodeLayoutHeight`), never from the DOM: out of the source port, down to a run `LOOP_ARC_DROP` (28 px) below the lowest of every body card's bottom and both port anchors, left, and up into `next`, the drop `LOOP_ARC_STUB` (24 px) right of the rightmost body card and the rise as far left of the leftmost, with the corners rounded (`LOOP_ARC_RADIUS`, 10 px). The body is the TARGET, the lane it owns and the wire's source, so neither vertical leg can cross a body card whatever order the lane is drawn in. Both canvases draw it through `loopArcOf` in `targetSummary.ts` (`FlowWireLayer.tsx`, next's `FlowWires.tsx`), for every event wire into a TARGET's `next`. The label chip, `every pass: next panel · 6 panels`, is drawn on the block's real loop wire only (`loopChip`): over a mid-lane wire (M12), a wire from another block's lane or one into a one-panel block it would promise a rotation the run does not make. Its count is the compile answer's entry for the block, rows times cols less the skipped panels, left off while the draft is dirty or while the entry's grid is no longer the block's, because the editor compiled when a flow was opened and when DONE or LOOP PANELS wrote through `flowsApplyFraming` (2.5), and after no other edit and no save (#356); since S5 a save compiles too (below). The dash is `LOOP_ARC_DASH` ("10 4 2 4"), a dash-dot no other wire draws, and it does not change while the run is live, which is when the night palette makes every lane one red, so the arc is told apart by that silhouette and its chip. next's remove control sits on the arc's `handle`, a point on its drop; until S5 the classic one sat at the midpoint of the two anchors, off the arc (#355), and on the classic phone FLOW tab the arc's legs were clipped at both edges and its run crossed the REPORT card (#360). The editor's rule for adding the wire is built in `panelLane.ts` (`loopSource`, `withLoop`: from the tail only, never a second one, and lifted from every stage when the loop is turned off), `flowsApplyFraming` applies it (2.5), and `test_flows_panel_lane.py` grades the graphs it makes against the compile (the fixture's `loop_cases`). The first wave did not build the phone stage list's rail and LOOP PANELS, or the modal's DONE; the second built all three (below, and 2.5), and the wizard's sheet was left to S6 (below).

**As built, the rail and LOOP PANELS** (S4, #189). The phone stage list draws the loop as the design's rail (`stageBlocks` in `FlowStagesPhoneSheet.tsx`): for a TARGET whose panels rotate (`targetLoops`), its row, then its panel lane (`panelLane`) indented behind a `2px dashed var(--warn)` line, ending at the lane's tail (`laneTail`), the stage the wire leaves, labelled `EVERY PASS: NEXT PANEL` (`LOOP_RAIL_LABEL`, the chip's words in the list's capitals). The lane is gathered under its TARGET rather than drawn where the list's breadth-first order puts it, so a stage the TARGET's own parent feeds (a DOME opened by the same DUSK) is never drawn inside the rail; with no looped mosaic the list is row for row what it was. With the wire absent, LOOP PANELS takes the rail's place: on the phone list under the TARGET's row, and in the footer of both canvases' cards, below the ports, so no wire anchor moves. It is offered exactly when a press would add the wire, which is one rule for the offer and the press (`offersLoopPanels`: `withLoop(graph, id, true)` hands back new wires), so it is never shown on a mosaic that already loops, a single target, a block that owns no stage, or a lane that branches or ends on a stage with no "pass done", and a press is `flowsApplyFraming(id, {}, true)`. The classic phone card has no footer, so it has no LOOP PANELS; there the loop is added by tap-to-wire or the modal's RUN toggle.

**As built since S5, the loop wire** (S5, #410, #355, #360, #356). LOOP PANELS over a lane whose loop wire is stranded mid-lane moves that wire rather than adding a second one (`withLoop`, #410): the first pass wire into the block from mid-lane is re-sourced to the tail, keeping its id and its place among the wires, any other is dropped, or every one when the tail already carries its own, and a pass wire from another block's lane is left alone. Added beside the stranded one, as S4 did, the new wire left the flow M12, the doctor asking for the loop at the tail just looped, and the card reading "rotate" over a flow `/run` refuses. The card now asks for no pass wire from mid-lane as well (`targetLoops`, over `midLanePassWires`), so the footer, the chip, the rail and the LOOP PANELS offer say what the run does, one panel at a time, until the lane has one loop; until S7 the modal's RUN toggle took its opening state from `loopWires` alone, so over such a lane it opened on (#429). The classic remove control sits on the arc's `handle` too, from the one resolver both it and the wire layer draw with (`wireLoopArc`, #355). On the classic phone FLOW tab the arc is drawn inside the column layout (`loopArcOf` in its `phone-flow` mode, #360): its legs stand `COLUMN_LOOP_STUB` (7 px, half of `AUTO_PAD`) outside the cards, and its run lies in the middle of the gap the layout leaves under a card (`COLUMN_LOOP_DROP`), clear of every card it lays out, so it takes the gap under the tail, above REPORT; until S7 the 26 px remove control centred on a leg 7 px inside the edge was clipped by 6 px (#428). A save compiles now (#356): once a save has written its answer, the editor compiles the graph the store then holds, which is the draft on screen when an edit landed while the save was in flight, and the answer records the graph it was asked about, which `compiledIsCurrent` compares with the one on screen; until S7 the chip withheld its count by `dirty`, not by `compiledIsCurrent`, so it showed the old count for one round trip after a save (below). The wizard's sheet (S6, #196) calls no `withLoop`: the server's generator draws the loop wire into the flow it writes (`_wire_the_loop`).

**As built since S7, the loop wire** (S7, #356, #429, #428). Both wire layers ask `compiledIsCurrent` and hand `loopChip` its negation as `stale`, where they handed it `dirty` (`FlowWireLayer.tsx`, next's `FlowWires.tsx`), so the chip withholds its count exactly while the compile answer in hand came from a graph other than the one on screen: a skip edited and saved no longer shows the old count for a round trip, and the count the modal's DONE compiles for the unsaved draft shows at once. The modal's RUN toggle opens from `targetLoops`, the reader the card, the chip, the rail and LOOP PANELS ask, so over a lane with a mid-lane pass wire beside its loop it opens off (#429); the tail's own loop wire is a separate fact (`tailWire`), so a looped mosaic made one panel still has its wire lifted at DONE. On the classic phone FLOW tab the remove control sits `LOOP_CONTROL_INSET_AUTO` (6 px) in from the arc's drop, so its 26 px box ends at the container's edge and is no longer clipped (#428); until H4 it covered 12 px of a tail card in the right column at the handle's height (#502, fixed in H4, below).

**As built since H4, the #/next run and the phone control** (H4, #357, #502). The #/next TARGET card is drawn taller than the formula's `cardBox`: a footer that wraps, the progress pill and the stage buttons under the lane reach `CARD_OVERHANG_PX` (101 px) below it, a number `FlowNode.tsx` computes from the sizes beside the card's classes less `nodeLayoutHeight` of a card with no rows, so a run `LOOP_ARC_DROP` below the formula crossed the card as drawn, the #/next half of #357. `nextLoopArc` in next's `FlowWires.tsx` still resolves the arc through `loopArcOf`, and lowers its run by that overhang (`geometry.lowerLoopRun`), so the run keeps its 28 px below the card as drawn: on the eighth Example the run is at 332 px, where it was at 231, 28 below the FILTER CYCLE card, whose drawn bottom is at 304. The layer and the #/next remove control read the same arc; the classic arc is unchanged, byte for byte. On the classic phone FLOW tab the remove control is placed at a spot on the arc that meets no card (`phoneLoopSpot`, over `geometry.loopControlSpot` and the card boxes `placedCards` gives), and falls back to #428's place only where no such spot exists, which a probe of 4000 column layouts never reached (#502). Three card states still reach past the 101 px, filed as #554: a marks row that wraps, a selected TARGET that offers both LOOP PANELS and EDIT STAGE, and a footer of four lines (`loopArcNext.test.tsx`, `loopArc.test.ts`, `flowWireDeleteLoop.test.tsx`). The issue's other way out, recording the #/next overlap as accepted rather than lowering the run, is the owner's to choose (owner list item 59).

### 1.5 The panel lane and stage ownership (the scoping rule)

Today every CAPTURE or CYCLE step is appended to every target seen earlier in topological order (`compile.py:216-259`, the two `for t in targets` loops at `:233` and `:258`). That rule is kept byte for byte for any graph with no multi-panel block, so all seven Examples and every saved flow compile unchanged.

When the graph contains any multi-panel TARGET block, **every** capture and cycle stage in that graph is instead scoped by wires, with these definitions:

1. **Lane nodes** are AUTOFOCUS, GUIDE, legacy SLEW, CAPTURE LOOP and FILTER CYCLE. Every other node with a flow port ends a lane: TARGET, POOL, DOME, DUSK FLATS, REPORT, DUSK.
2. **The panel lane** of a TARGET (or POOL) is every lane node reachable from its flow output through lane nodes only. Because every node has at most one flow input, walking back from any node gives exactly one chain.
3. **The owner** of a stage is found by `owner_of(graph, node_id) -> FlowNode | None`, a new pure function in `compile.py`. It walks the single flow parent of the stage, then of that parent, through lane nodes only. It returns the first TARGET or POOL it reaches, and `None` when it reaches any other node type or runs out of parents. A seen-set bounds the walk.
   - This is **not** the doctor's walk. `_flow_upstream_types` (`doctor.py:40-63`) collects the set of every type upstream, which answers "did the cursor pass a guider?" and cannot name a nearest owner. It stays for those presence questions. Compile, to_plan and the doctor rules M3, M4, M12 and M13 call `owner_of`.
4. **The tail** of a multi-panel block's lane is its last stage: the stage whose `complete` output feeds no lane node. A multi-panel lane must be a single chain, with no fan-out from the TARGET or between lane nodes. A branched lane is M12 (danger, not runnable), because "the last stage" would be ambiguous.
5. **The loop wire leaves the tail.** Modal DONE, LOOP PANELS, the wizard and every generator wire `pass` from the tail. A pass wire from any earlier stage of the same lane is M12.
6. **What runs next** is whatever the tail's "all done" output feeds. By construction that is never a stage: a CAPTURE wired straight after the tail is itself a lane node, so it is part of the panel lane and becomes the new tail. The editor moves the loop wire to the new tail when a stage is appended, the move 1.4 names (S3, #189): `carryLoopWire` in `flowsSlice.ts`, in the connect's own graph write, and only when the new wire leaves the lane's tail, the block is multi-panel, the lane already has a loop wire and the new tail has a `pass` output. The moved wire keeps its id, and a new tail that already carries a loop wire of its own keeps it while the old one is removed. Until S4 a stage with no `pass` output (AUTOFOCUS, GUIDE) appended after the tail became the tail and left the loop wire where it was, mid-lane (M12), and a stage appended after it did not carry the wire either, because the tail it was drawn from had no loop wire, so the mosaic could not run until the operator rewired it by hand (#331). Since S4 both have a `pass` output (1.3), so the carry reaches them, and the wire it moves is every pass wire into the block's `next` from any stage of the lane, not only the one from the tail the operator drew from (S4, #331): a flow saved with its loop stranded mid-lane opens that way, and the next append carries the wire to the new tail. Legacy SLEW is the one lane type left without the port: appended after the tail it keeps the wire where it was, mid-lane, and the compile refuses that (M12). When the port was added, the doctor's M3, M4 and M12 still read only CAPTURE LOOP and FILTER CYCLE pass wires (#375); M4's note on a wire the compile consumes into a one-panel block read the compile's list from #389 (1.8), and since S5 all three read the vocabulary's `PASS_TYPES`, as the compile does (S5, #375): M12 names a mid-lane pass wire from an AUTOFOCUS or GUIDE, M4 warns of one from another block's lane, and M3 names a lane that ends on either as the stage to wire the loop from.
7. **A stage with no owner** in a graph that has a multi-panel block is M13 (danger, not runnable). For example: TARGET -> CYCLE -> DOME -> CAPTURE. The CAPTURE walks back to DOME, which ends the lane, so the CAPTURE belongs to no target. Under the legacy rule it would have been appended to every panel, which is C's leak. Under this rule it would compile to nothing, and that must be loud. It needs its own TARGET.

Worked cases:

| Graph (flow wires) | Panels own | Tail | Next |
|---|---|---|---|
| TARGET(3x2) -> AF -> GUIDE -> CYCLE -> REPORT | CYCLE | CYCLE | REPORT |
| TARGET(3x2) -> CYCLE -> CAPTURE Ha -> REPORT | CYCLE, CAPTURE | CAPTURE | REPORT |
| TARGET(3x2) -> CYCLE -> TARGET(M33) -> CAPTURE | CYCLE | CYCLE | TARGET M33, which owns the CAPTURE |
| TARGET(3x2) -> CYCLE -> DOME -> CAPTURE | CYCLE | CYCLE | DOME; CAPTURE is M13 |

The switch covers the whole graph, and that closes C's leak. Whenever the two rules would differ for a stage, the compile adds a note that names the stage. The legacy rule is filed as its own defect (I-05).

**As built** (S3, #189). The rule is in `compile.py`, pure: `owner_of`, `panel_lane`, `lane_branched`, `lane_tail`, `lane_next`, `loop_wires` and `is_multi_panel`, one set of answers for compile, to_plan and the doctor to share, and `compile_plan` scopes by them. Where the definitions above are silent, the code decides. A wire is a flow wire only when it is one at both ends, and one parent wired twice is one parent. `owner_of` answers `None` for a node that is not itself a lane node, and for a stage with two flow parents, which validation refuses but the compile routes still see. The lane is exactly the lane nodes `owner_of` gives the block, nearest first and then by canvas position, so the lane the doctor checks is the lane the compile scopes. It branches when two of its nodes stand at the same distance from the block, and a sibling that is not a lane node (a DOME) branches nothing. The tail is its last node, of any type, AUTOFOCUS and GUIDE included (neither had a `pass` output until S4 gave them one, 1.3), and there is none for an empty or a branched lane. `loop_wires` is structural, leaving the 1x1 note (M4) to its caller. A grid side that is missing, is not a whole number, or is below 1 reads as 1. The compile's note is an entry in a new top-level `notes` list of the compiled plan, `{node_id, level, text}`: level `note` for a stage narrowed to its own block, `warn` for one that belongs to no block and so compiles to nothing. The key is absent when the list is empty, so a graph with at most one TARGET or POOL block, whatever its panel count, compiles byte for byte as it did before S3 once the keys S3 added to every entry are removed (S4, #189; narrowed from "no multi-panel block" by the W2 backlog's WP-19(a), #151's general case, which scopes any graph with more than one owner block by its wires whether or not either is a mosaic): a TARGET entry's `angle`, `mosaic`, `loop`, `centre`, `count_mode` and `frame_anchor` (3.2), and a POOL member's `count_mode`. The worked cases above are what these functions compute, and `test_flows_panel_lane.py` holds them, with more, in a fixture the TypeScript mirror can share.

### 1.6 What "done" means and what runs next

**The group is done** when every non-skipped panel's every step meets its count in the plan's count mode. That is exactly `Session.owed()` over the group's steps equalling 0 (`session.py:143-152`), the single definition of finished. The session is stamped complete only when `owed() == 0` over the whole plan. Otherwise it goes dormant and stays armed (`engine.py:1717-1816`).

**What runs next.** Next is whatever the tail's "all done" output is wired to (1.5, item 6). Targets reachable from it are **followers** of the group. While the group has live members, followers sort after every member in `remaining`, whatever their window start. The flow's **`whenWaiting` setting** decides what happens while a mosaic cannot be shot. It is one option per flow, stored in `FlowGraph.settings` (Revision 2, ruling 1), and it applies to every multi-panel block in that flow:

- **"Shoot later targets, then come back"** (the default, by the owner's ruling of 2026-09-24: "No sense in wasting time due to an obstruction"). While every live panel is waiting (below its floor, behind the horizon mask, held by the meridian rule, or in the group's deferral wait), the scheduler may select a ready follower. The deferral wait is one of those waits since S3 (S3, #304). S2 built it as one `_wait_until` of `DEFER_WAIT_S` inside `_close_group_pass`, so a follower waited it out with the group; now the pass boundary only sets the wait's end (`GroupRun.defer_next_pass`), and `_eligibility_now` holds each member as a waiter until then (5.1). This is today's window-sorted skip-ahead (`engine.py:1924-1938`), with one change: such a follower runs with `VisitBound(deadline_ts = group_ready_ts)`.
  - `group_ready_ts` is the earliest real time a live member becomes eligible: its meridian crossing (5.7), the projected time a blocked panel clears the floor and the mask, or the opening its gating waits for (`_group_ready_ts`). During a deferral wait (5.1) no member is eligible before it ends, so `group_ready_ts` is the later of the earliest of those times and the wait's end (S3, #304), and a follower picked in the wait hands the cursor back when the next pass may begin. The projected clearing time is a forward scan in 60 s steps over the same predicate as `_mount_floor_verdict`, in the shape of `_time_to_gate` (`schedule.py:772`). A member with no clearing time tonight does not bound it. The 60 s recheck is a re-evaluation cadence, never a deadline.
  - The deadline is checked at frame boundaries in `_run_steps`, never inside `_run_step`. For a follower with blocks acquisition that means calling `_run_step(max_frames=1)` in a loop, which is the path cycle acquisition already takes, and checking the deadline between calls.
  - At the deadline the follower's visit ends. It keeps its progress, is requeued behind the group, and selection returns to the group.
  - Once the group is set aside for tonight, or complete, a follower runs its normal course.
  - Without this bound, a follower selected at any all-waiting moment runs to completion and is removed (`engine.py:1964-1965`, `:2010`). The routine pre-flip idle (5.7: 0 to 10.6 min with cut visits, and 2.4 to 37.3 min without them, in the Appendix A.4 cases), a tree, or one 60 s recheck would hand the rest of the night away at every meridian crossing.
- **"Wait for the mosaic"**. Followers get `Target.after_group = <group id>`. Gating reads them as waiting ("after the M31 mosaic") while the group has live members. They are skipped for the night, not marked done, once the group is set aside tonight. They become ready when the group is complete. Tonight shows how many idle hours this choice costs.

Default and why: a multi-night mosaic that holds its followers would idle clear sky for weeks, and a follower that keeps the cursor would starve the mosaic at every meridian crossing. The bounded follower visit gives neither. The owner's "before moving on" is met on any night the mosaic can be shot, because a follower only fills gaps the mosaic cannot use and hands the cursor back when a panel is due again. The owner chose this default on 2026-09-24 and asked for it to be an option; "Wait for the mosaic" stays one tap away.

**Where the option lives.** `FlowGraph` gains `settings: dict` of flat scalars, with missing-key defaults in one table (`FLOW_SETTINGS` in `flows/models.py`, mirrored in `flowsTypes.ts` and covered by the parity test). Its first key is `whenWaiting`, missing-key default "Shoot later targets, then come back". There is no older meaning to preserve, because no graph had a multi-panel block before S3, and the setting does nothing in a graph without one. It is edited in three places, all writing the same key: the flow overview (the inspector with nothing selected), the modal's RUN section (labelled "for every mosaic in this flow"), and the wizard's mosaic path (#196). The compile copies it into each block's `mosaic.when_waiting` (3.2), so the engine sees it per group as before.

**As built** (S3, #189). `FlowGraph.settings` and `FLOW_SETTINGS` are built, `whenWaiting` with the owner's default, and the compile copies it into every block's `mosaic.when_waiting`. The compile also marks each follower: a TARGET or POOL entry that a multi-panel block's tail feeds, directly or further down the flow lane, carries `follows`, that block's node id (3.2). One downstream of two mosaics follows the nearer, a block that owns no stage is its own tail, and a branched lane has no followers (`_followers`). `to_plan` gives a follower `after_group` only when the flow says "Wait for the mosaic" (`WAIT_FOR_THE_MOSAIC`) and its mosaic made a group; under the default, and after a mosaic whose every panel is skipped, it gets no field.

**As built** (S4, #330). A follower that is itself a mosaic waits too. `to_plan` writes the gate on every panel of such a follower, and until S4 the engine read a gate only on a target in no group, so under "Wait for the mosaic" the downstream mosaic shot through the upstream one's waits (3.5). A member's `after_group` now holds its whole group, the reading the start paths' cycle refusal already gave: the group is done only when every panel is, so the other panels shooting while the gated one waited would shoot the mosaic before the one it follows all the same. `_start_groups` gathers what each group waits for from every member (`_group_gates`), leaving out a gate on the member's own group and one on a group the plan does not carry, and `_group_gate` answers from those groups, through the rule a follower's own gate uses (`_after_group_verdict`): skipped for the night once any of them is set aside tonight, since that one cannot be completed tonight and the gate could never open; waiting while any can still shoot; ready once every one is complete. A held group is asked nothing else: `_eligibility_now` reads every group's gating first and then gives verdicts, and gives a held group's members no reach, meridian or deferral verdict, so no pier is read and no panel is set aside for a group that could not have been shot, and `_group_ready_ts` gives it no time, so a follower is not left without room by a group that is not due. A held member is a waiter with no wake time of its own, and the line "M33: waits: after the M31 mosaic" is said once per pair, flagged site-derived as a follower's is. A skipped group leaves the night whole, not done and not recorded (`_skip_group_tonight`): the records that set the group it waits for aside already say why, and a restart tonight reads them and skips it again. The selection is then asked again over what is left, so a panel of the skipped group is never the waiter the run sleeps on (#379). Until S5 the skip was decided only once a member was ready, as a follower's was, so a set-aside mosaic's follower kept the run alive, idle, until its own window opened (#374). Since S5 the selection asks the gate of a target still waiting on its window or its altitude too, for the skip alone (`_follower_gate`'s `waiting`), so such a target leaves the night the moment the mosaic it waits for is set aside, a member with its whole group; the wait and ready verdicts, and the "waits:" line, stay with the moment it is ready (S5, #374).

**As built, the overview** (S4, #189). The flow overview, the inspector with nothing selected, edits `whenWaiting` in both editors. The classic one is a select labelled "While a mosaic waits (for every mosaic in this flow)" (`WHEN_WAITING_OVERVIEW_LABEL` in `FlowInspector.tsx`), a copy of the RUN section's `WHEN_WAITING_LABEL` that a test holds equal, because importing the modal's constant would pull the modal into the editor's chunk. The #/next one is a segmented control headed WHILE A MOSAIC WAITS (`WHEN_WAITING_TITLE` in `FlowInspectorColumn.tsx`), whose accessible name says it is for every mosaic in the flow, locked with its reason on a read-only Example as the flow's name is. Both show the value the run will use, a missing or unknown one read as the default (`flowSetting`), and both write through `flowsSetSetting`, which keeps every other key of `settings` and refuses a value `FLOW_SETTINGS` does not offer, and a key the table does not own (`Object.hasOwn`): indexed plainly, an inherited `toString` threw out of the store action where its contract says `false` (#397). Since a second verification of the third wave's tasks, a case holds the #/next overview's default for a flow with no setting and for a value this build does not know (S4, #400); the classic select shows the default for an unknown value whichever value it reads, because a select whose value matches no option shows its first and `FLOW_SETTINGS` lists the default first, so its case grades what is shown, not the read (S4, #401). Neither compiles, as a name edit does not, so the compile that copies the setting into each block's `mosaic.when_waiting` comes with the next compile the editor makes (#356). While no TARGET in the flow has more than one panel, each says under the control that the setting changes nothing yet (`WHEN_WAITING_NO_MOSAIC`, in each editor's own words). With the modal's RUN section (2.4) that is two of the three places above; the wizard's mosaic path is #196's, which S4 did not build (section 8's S4), and S6 built the wizard's sheet with no such row (S6, #196), so a flow the wizard makes takes the default until one of the other two edits it.

### 1.7 SLEW + CENTER becomes part of TARGET

- `slew` stays in `NODE_DEFS`, so saved graphs load, but it moves to a new `LEGACY_TYPES` set that is hidden from the palette.
- `PALETTE_COVERS_EVERY_NODE_TYPE` (`ui/src/components/flows/palette.ts:144-146`) becomes "covers every non-legacy type". The pins in `nodeDefs.test.ts` and `palette.test.ts:48` change deliberately: still 21 types, 1 of them legacy.
- **No graph rewrite.** Folding SLEW out of stored graphs would have to happen on raw dicts, because `FlowNode._known_type` rejects an unknown type before `with_defaults` runs (`flows/models.py:60-65`). Leaving the node in place avoids that risk entirely.
- A legacy SLEW's `tol`, `retries` and `solver` are **not** carried to TARGET. They never reached the run (`to_plan.py:361-377`). What actually ran was the hub default of 0.02 deg with 3 attempts (`hub.py:6387-6391`). Carrying `tol 0.5` would silently tighten every saved flow's centring.
- Doctor note L1, filled from the node's own values: "this stage is part of the TARGET block now. Its 0.5 arcmin tolerance never reached the run, which centred to 1.2 arcmin. Delete it and set centring on the TARGET."
- Doctor rule 4 (`doctor.py:167-170`) becomes: "CAPTURE with no TARGET upstream - the loop shoots wherever the mount happens to point". It is satisfied by a TARGET, a POOL, or a legacy SLEW upstream along wires.
- The wizard lane (`wizard.py:266-281`), the brief sentence (`tonight.py:664-666`) and the Examples drop SLEW in slice S3. A test pins that their compiled plans are unchanged.

**As built** (S3, #189). `LEGACY_TYPES` is `{"slew"}` in `nodes.py`, mirrored in `nodeDefs.ts`: `NODE_DEFS` still holds 21 types, `PALETTE_GROUPS` offers each of the other 20 once, and `PALETTE_COVERS_EVERY_NODE_TYPE` also fails the type check when the palette offers a legacy type. L1 is filled from the node's own `tol` and from the centring the run used, TARGET's missing-key `centerTol`, which is the hub's 0.02 deg: "SLEW + CENTER - this stage is part of the TARGET block now. Its 0.5 arcmin tolerance never reached the run, which centred to 1.2 arcmin. Delete it and set centring on the TARGET." R4 is 1.8's. `wizard.py`'s lanes and the seven Examples no longer draw SLEW, each block wired straight to the stage after it, and `brief` has no SLEW sentence, so a legacy SLEW left on a canvas is named by L1 alone. Both UIs' START BLANK drew one until the integration of S3, beside a TARGET made from the missing-key defaults, so a blank flow opened with L1's note; it now draws a TARGET and a CAPTURE LOOP, each made from the Created-as column (`BLANK_NODES` and `createParams` in `FlowWizard.tsx` and `wizardModel.ts`, #340), so a blank flow opens with no legacy stage and a TARGET that names no object (`blankFlow.test.ts`). Dropping it moved their plans by the count mode only, which a created TARGET sets to accepted subs (ruling 2): every three-answer wizard plan, and each of the seven Examples' plans, is the plan the pre-S3 graph compiles to once its `count_mode` is set back to attempts (`test_flows_wizard.py`, `test_flows_examples_s3.py`).

### 1.8 Doctor rules added or changed

The wording follows the house rules: explain why, no em-dashes, never the word "slave" (`doctor.py:3-21`). There is no UI copy of the doctor to update: no rule text exists anywhere in `ui/src`, and the UI renders the issues the server returns. The docstring at `doctor.py:13-17` said otherwise, and S3 corrected it (S3, #169).

| Id | Level | Fires when | Text (abridged) |
|---|---|---|---|
| R4 | warn | capture or cycle has no TARGET, POOL or legacy SLEW upstream along wires (graphs with no multi-panel block; with one, M13 replaces it) | "CAPTURE with no TARGET upstream - the loop shoots wherever the mount happens to point" |
| M1 | danger, plus `GraphNotRunnable` | multi-panel block with fovX or fovY = 0 | "Frame this block: panels are tiled from the camera's field, and this block has not recorded one." |
| M2 | danger, plus `GraphNotRunnable` | multi-panel block with angle "Any angle" | "A mosaic is laid out at one camera angle, and with no angle the panels will not tile. Lock an angle, or use the angle the camera measured." |
| M3 | warn | multi-panel block owns a stage but has no loop wire | "Panels are shot one after another: a night cut short leaves the last panels empty. Wire FILTER CYCLE 'pass done' to TARGET 'next panel' to rotate panels every pass." (names the tail) |
| M4 | note / warn | loop wire on a 1x1 block (note) / from a stage in another block's lane, by `owner_of` (warn) | "one panel, nothing to rotate between" / "this wire does nothing: FILTER CYCLE is not in this TARGET's panel lane" |
| M5 | warn, then loss | live effective optics differ from the snapshot by more than 2% (warn). Live field smaller than the step `fov x (1 - overlap)` on either axis (loss: blocks `/run` until accepted) | "framed for 2.00 x 1.33 deg; this camera now images 1.40 x 0.93 deg, so the panels would leave gaps. Re-frame." |
| M6 | warn | meridian convergence between neighbouring panels uses more than 25% of their overlap (Appendix A) | "at Dec 75 meridian convergence turns neighbouring panels against each other, which uses 38.9% of their 10% overlap. Widen the overlap or use fewer columns." (the 4x1, 4 columns by 1 row, at 10% at Dec 75, at angle 0; S4 orchestrator ruling 1) |
| M7 | warn, plus loss | two blocks disagree on `counts` (count_mode is one plan-wide setting, `sequence/models.py:309`) | "this plan counts accepted subs because TARGET M31 asks for it; TARGET M33's 'every sub taken' cannot be honoured in the same run" |
| M8 | warn / note | "Rotate to PA" with no rotator in the active profile (warn) / "Camera fixed at PA" (note) | "no rotator: turn the camera by hand to PA 30.0 before the first panel. The run measures the angle at every panel and holds the mosaic if it is off by more than 6.0 deg." (the combined budget of Appendix A.2) |
| M9 | warn | counts accepted, no stop boundary, and both reject guards off | previews the `quota_unbounded` refusal (`sequence/models.py:402-435`) before Run |
| M10 | note | a measured hop cost is more than 25% of a visit (only when a measured cost is injected) | "each hop costs 2 m 40 s (measured over 6 hops) against a 4 min visit; 2 passes per visit would cut hops by half" (a 3x2 on a 4-minute LRGB pass) |
| M11 | danger | two blocks share a name | "two blocks are both called M31; panel names and rules find targets by name" |
| M12 | danger, plus `GraphNotRunnable` | a multi-panel lane branches, or a pass wire leaves a stage of the lane that is not its tail (1.5) | "the loop wire starts at FILTER CYCLE, but CAPTURE Ha comes after it in the panel lane. Start the loop wire at CAPTURE Ha to shoot it on every panel, or give CAPTURE Ha its own TARGET." |
| M13 | danger, plus `GraphNotRunnable` | in a graph with a multi-panel block, a capture or cycle stage has no owner by `owner_of` (1.5, item 7) | "CAPTURE Ha belongs to no TARGET: DOME ends the M31 panel lane. It would shoot nothing. Give it a TARGET." |
| M14 | note | a REPORT "target done" wire leaves a lane owned by a multi-panel block | "SESSION REPORT 'target done' fires once per panel, not once for the mosaic: TARGET M31 has 6 panels, so what the wire triggers runs once for each panel finished" (I-35) |
| M15 | danger | the angle budget is spent: meridian convergence alone uses half the overlap (Appendix A.2, `k <= 0`) | "at Dec {dec} meridian convergence alone uses {c}% of the overlap, which leaves no room for camera angle error. Widen the overlap or use fewer columns." (filled from the A.1 computation; the 4x1 at 10% at Dec 75 uses 38.9% and does not trip it) |
| L1 | note | legacy SLEW present | see 1.7 |

**As built** (S3, #189). R4, M1 to M15 and L1 are in `doctor.py`. `check(graph, *, standards=None, mount=None, rig=None)` runs M1 to M15 after the older rules, so a flow saved before S3 reads its issues in the order it always did, and L1 last. `rig` is a `RigFacts` (`flows/rig.py`): the live field, a measured hop and how many hops it was measured over (the engine answers both through `measured_cost`, `None` for a kind it has not measured and never its seed), whether the profile has a rotator and whether both reject guards are off, each `None` when unknown, and a rule that needs a fact (M5, M8, M9, M10) is silent without it. The lane rules ask the compile's functions (1.5); M6 and M15 read `framing.convergence_share` and `ROTATION_BUDGET`, and M8's figure is `framing.angle_tolerance_deg`, so the doctor keeps no copy of Appendix A. Where the table is silent the code decides. M15 replaces M6 once convergence spends the budget, since the two would say one thing twice. M2 also fires for a set angle with a negative rotation, which 3.1 reads as none. M8's note for a fixed camera needs the rotator fact too. M11, M13 and M14 speak only in a graph with a multi-panel block, so a flow saved before S3 draws exactly the issues it drew before; M4, M7 and M9 read values only an S3 build writes, and speak anywhere. M6, M15 and M8's figure measure only a block with typed coordinates (`identity.typed_coordinates`). M9 reads its stop boundary from `compile_plan`'s schedule, so a "Clock time" stop left blank on the card counts as none, the same as no stop at all, and a "Clock time" stop the card actually gives a time counts as a real boundary (amended 2026-09-30 by backlog WP-09, #191). M10's visit is `max(passes, ceil(minVisit / pass))` passes of shutter, with no overhead assumed. M14 counts the grid, skipped panels included. M5 only warns here; its loss is `to_plan`'s (3.3). The M6, M10 and M14 texts above are the doctor's own, for the cases named beside them; the design's M10 example, a hop of 2 m 40 s against a 13 min visit, is 20.5% of it, under the rule's own 25%. The routes hand the doctor the rig facts (3.3), the compile route the one object it compiles the plan with, so M5, M8, M9 and M10 speak in the product. The wizard route (`POST /api/flows/wizard`) injects the camera field and the rotator the same way, and the angle the last solve measured (`hub.last_sky_angle`) for USE MEASURED, as the two bullets below ask; it answers the generator's `notes`, and a mosaic answer the generator refuses is a 422 (`invalid_wizard_answer`).

**As built** (S4, #349, #339, S4 orchestrator rulings 1 and 3). M4's note is given for exactly the wires the compile consumes as nothing, the list `one_panel_pass_wires` answers (1.4, S4 orchestrator ruling 3), so the note and the missing loss cannot name two sets; a pass wire into a one-panel block from another block's lane keeps M4's warning, and the compile still reports it. M4 walks that list beside its own `pass_wires`, in wire order, because until S5 `pass_wires` read only CAPTURE LOOP and FILTER CYCLE (#375) while the compile's list read the vocabulary's `PASS_TYPES`: walked over `pass_wires` alone, once AUTOFOCUS and GUIDE gained the port (1.3), a one-panel wire from either was consumed with no note, as though the doctor had nothing to say about it (#389). Since S5 `pass_wires` and M3's tail read `compile.PASS_TYPES` too (S5, #375), so M3, M4 and M12 speak of every pass wire the compile reads, and M4 still walks both lists, so that its note follows the compile's consumption whatever `pass_wires` reads. M6's worked case and its constant's comment write the grid columns by rows, "4x1 (4 columns by 1 row)" (S4 orchestrator ruling 1), and the sentence's remedy, fewer columns, is about the four side by side. The doctor's number reader reads an integer past a float's range as no number (#328), as the compile's does (3.2).

The wizard must still produce output that passes the doctor at note level or better across its option matrix (`wizard.py:9-15`). A wizard mosaic therefore always carries an angle, a camera field and the loop wire, and the wizard must be able to supply the first two honestly:

- **Angle.** The wizard's mosaic kind asks for it, or offers USE MEASURED from `status.sky_angle` (`hub.py:7001`). It never writes a hard-coded default: a default angle nobody chose is exactly the I-04 defect (23.4 commanding a rotator).
- **Camera field.** The wizard route injects the live effective optics (`hub.effective_optics`, `hub.py:1987`) as a rig fact, as `to_plan` does for `cool_to`. With no optics it answers with a single target and says why ("set the camera and focal length in Settings > Optics to plan a mosaic"). It never emits an M1 graph.

**As built since S6, the door's answers** (S6, #196). The wizard route (`FlowWizardBody`) takes what Send to Flow Wizard pre-fills beside the three answers it always took: `ra` and `dec` as typed, the framing's centre and not the catalogue's, `skip`, the filter rows as a FILTER CYCLE's slot table (`cycle_plan`) with `cycles`, and `guiding`. Each is None when not given and then changes nothing, so a body with only the three answers generates exactly what it did (Revision 2, ruling 4). It injects one more rig fact, the connected wheel's usable slots (`_rig_wheel`), which the rows are checked against. `cycles` and `guiding` are read before pydantic's coercion (`checked_cycles`, `checked_guiding`), so `true` is not one pass and "true" is not guiding, and a skip is read against the grid it names, optics or none (`checked_skip`). The generator refuses in words what it cannot honour: a coordinate without the other (`_door_coords`), a row it cannot read whole, a filter the wheel does not hold, or rows with no `cycles` (`_door_rows`), `guiding` false beside a lit Guiding chip (`_door_guiding`), and an unguided row longer than the unguided sub cap (`_within_the_unguided_cap`). Until S7 that cap was accepted up to 3600 s, so from 120 s the wizard wrote a sub the doctor's rule 2 warns about (#432). Since S7 the cap is taken only below the doctor's line, `UNGUIDED_SUB_LINE_S` (120 s), the one copy of the number, which rule 2 reads too: the route's body bounds `unguided_exposure_s` under it, and the generator refuses an answer at or past it on every lane, in words naming the field (`_unguided_seconds`, 422 `invalid_wizard_answer`), rather than shorten it, while a NaN reads as the default (S7, #432). Until H4 an unguided narrowband quick flow wrote 180 s subs at its generator's own defaults, the same class (#518). Since H4 (H4, #518, H4 orchestrator ruling 5) the quick flow refuses, with a 422 and before it generates, an unguided flow any of whose subs is at or past the line (`_within_the_unguided_line`): the refusal names the limit and every filter at or past it, the sub is judged as it would be written, after rounding, so a typed 119.6 is refused as 120, and the line is read from `doctor.UNGUIDED_SUB_LINE_S` when the flow is asked for. The #/next quick sheet shows the limit beside the Guide switch while Guide is off (`UNGUIDED_NOTE`, over its mirror of the line, `UNGUIDED_SUB_LINE_S`; `test_h4_quick_unguided_line.py`, `quickUnguidedLimit.test.tsx`). Since backlog WP-48a (#588, 2026-10-01) the classic quick flow's Guide toggle shows the same note while Guide is off, its own copy of `UNGUIDED_NOTE`/`UNGUIDED_SUB_LINE_S` in `QuickFlow.tsx`, held equal to the #/next sheet's and to `doctor.UNGUIDED_SUB_LINE_S` by a drift test (`w6QuickFlowUnguidedNote.test.tsx`), and meets the same 422 if Guide is left off past the line.

---

## 2. The Target modal ("FRAME")

### 2.1 Where it lives and how it opens

- **One shared component**: `ui/src/components/flows/framing/TargetFramingSheet.tsx`, plus a pure model file. The #/next tree re-exports it (the rebuild must not fork presentation files). It is loaded lazily, following the entry-chunk rule D-FU-2.
- **Classic desktop**: a `FRAME ON SKY` row in the per-type slot next to calib's (`ui/src/components/flows/FlowInspector.tsx:146`). No new `FieldDef` control is added, so the "three controls" pin holds (`nodeDefs.test.ts:406-462`). The plain field rows stay for keyboard editing.
- **#/next**: a lazy `flowFrame` sheet registered in `reg.ts`. On phone it opens **in place of** `flowNode` at depth 2, because a third sheet would replace the second (`ui/src/next/router.ts:47`, `:263-272`). It carries `?open=` like every flows sheet (`FlowsCanvasHost.tsx:109-137`). The rebuild's slot sits beside `calibSlot` (`inspector/FlowNodeEditor.tsx:84`).
- **Palette drop** opens the modal at once. This needs `flowsAddNode` to return the new id; today it returns nothing, and adding a stage does not select it (`FlowPalette.tsx:145-149`, `flowsSlice.ts:249-257`).
- **Read-only Examples** open it in view mode and say why.

**As built** (S4, #189). The one shared component is built: `TargetFramingSheet.tsx`, over the pure model `framingModel.ts` and a few presentational files beside it (`FramingSky.tsx`, `framingApi.ts`, `framing.css` and one file per section under `sections/`). It is loaded lazily through `index.ts`, whose `loadTargetFramingSheet` is the dynamic import a sheet registry entry takes as its `load` and `TargetFramingSheetLazy` the same module as a lazy component, and nothing imports the sheet's module statically. It takes `{nodeId, onClose, viewOnly?}`, carries the view marker `data-testid="target-framing-sheet"`, and says "That stage is no longer in this flow." (`GONE`) for a node that is gone or is not a TARGET. A read-only Example opens in view mode on its own, with CLOSE for CANCEL, no DONE, every control in one disabled fieldset, and the reason line "Example flow: its framing opens to view, not to edit. Duplicate the flow to frame your own." (`EXAMPLE_VIEW_ONLY`). The second wave built none of the classic FRAME ON SKY row, the #/next `flowFrame` sheet and the palette drop that opens the modal, so nothing mounted the sheet when it closed; the third built all three (below), the drop on the id `flowsAddNode` returns (2.5).

**As built, the doors** (S4, #189). Each UI mounts the one shared sheet through its lazy door, and neither forks it. In the classic editor `FlowEditor.tsx` hosts the one open modal: it renders `TargetFramingSheetLazy` inside a Suspense and an error boundary (`FramingBoundary`), so a chunk that fails to load or a sheet that throws says "The framing sheet could not open. The flow is still here: save it, then reload the page to try again." (`FRAMING_FAILED`) instead of reaching the view's boundary, whose reload would lose an unsaved graph. The FRAME ON SKY row is in a TARGET's per-type slot beside calib's, a row and not a `FieldDef`, with every plain field row kept below it; it reaches the host through `FramingDoorContext`, and outside an editor, with no host, there is no row. On a read-only Example it reads VIEW ON SKY, with the sheet's own reason under it before it is pressed (`EXAMPLE_FRAMING_VIEW_ONLY`, a copy a test holds equal to `EXAMPLE_VIEW_ONLY`, since importing the sheet's constant would pull the sheet into the editor's chunk). Opening the modal closes the edit sheet, so on a tablet or a phone the modal replaces it, as `flowFrame` replaces `flowNode`, rather than stacking two modal Overlays that would both trap focus and both close on one Escape. The modal is pinned to the flow it was opened in (`frame.recordId`), because node ids repeat across flows, and a key pressed in it no longer reaches the canvas behind it, whose window handler deleted the selection, which was the block being framed (#381). In #/next, `flowFrame` is registered through `framing/reg.ts`, whose entry names only a dynamic import, so the registry in the entry chunk pays for no part of the modal, and `FlowFrameSheet.tsx` adapts the router's sheet to the shared sheet, which it mounts through `TargetFramingSheetLazy` in a Suspense of its own. The door is built into `FlowNodeEditor` for every TARGET, so the docked column and the `flowNode` sheet both carry it, and it is never locked, since an Example opens in view mode. Every door passes `?open=`, the flow the store holds (`flowFrameParams`), and on a phone `nav.sheet` puts `flowFrame` in place of `flowNode` at depth 2. While `?open=` names a flow the store does not hold, the sheet mounts no modal: it shows a card that says it opens once that flow has loaded, with BACK (`FRAME_OTHER_FLOW` while another flow is open, `FRAME_FLOW_LOADING` while none is), and the modal mounts once that flow has loaded; before, a link to one flow's `flowFrame` framed the namesake node of the flow already open, and DONE would have written it onto the other (#384). The sheet's close pops the stack only while `flowFrame` is on top. That guard was built because the shared sheet called `onClose` a second time when DONE's compile answered after CANCEL (#382); since a second verification of the second wave's tasks the shared sheet refuses CANCEL, Escape and the scrim while DONE writes, and closes only while it is still mounted (S4, #382; 2.5), so the guard stays as defence in depth. A TARGET dropped from the palette opens its modal at once in both UIs, on the id the drop made, and any other stage opens nothing: the classic editor's `onPick` opens it through the same door, and #/next's through `frameDroppedStage`, which puts the modal in place of the palette sheet with no history entry (`openFlowFrame`) and pushes it from the docked rail. The rail does not pass `flowsAddNode`'s id on yet, so there `droppedStageId` reads the node the add appended, in the same synchronous handler. The drop does not select the new stage. Until S5 neither host passed run mode's `viewOnly`; since S5 both pass it while the flow's session runs, the classic editor through `FramingHostSheet` and #/next through `FlowFrameSheet.tsx` (S5, #189; 2.6).

**As built, the doors since their second runs** (S4, #382, #384, #400, #401). A second verification of the third wave's tasks and a second run of the fourth wave's probe held the doors further. Each close the classic host hands the sheet clears only its own opening (`closeFrame`), so a close that arrives after its modal was replaced, even by a second opening of the same block, closes nothing: the host's half of #382's guard, as the #/next sheet pops only while `flowFrame` is on top. Escape reaches that #/next guard only through the modal: `SheetHost` binds Escape to `nav.back()` on the window, which would pop the sheet whatever the modal decided, and the modal's Overlay takes the key on `document` in the capture phase and stops it there, so the shared sheet's refusal holds in #/next too (#382). The classic canvas asks at each press whether any modal Overlay is open, so a key pressed in the tablet's edit sheet or palette sheet, which cover the canvas as the modal does, is kept from it too, and a case now holds that (#401); so does one for the #/next door on a read-only Example, which opens the modal to view (#400). The `flowFrame` sheet's cards sit in its own frame (`.nx-sheet` and `.nx-sheet-body`), inside the gutter every sheet has, 358 px wide on the 390 px phone, 16 px from each side and 10 px from the top; without it the frame shrank to its card, which the probe's second run found on the phone's top-left corner, its border on the screen's edges (#384).

### 2.2 Layout

**Phone portrait (390 x 844 as the reference)**

1. **Header**, 48 px, sticky: `CANCEL`, `FRAME M31`, `DONE`. DONE is a HonestButton (section 2.4).
2. **Sky**, pinned outside the scroller (the Atlas scroll-trap lesson). Height `min(100vw, 52svh)`, which is 390 px on the reference phone. It shrinks to `40svh` while a text field has focus. The canvas is never hidden behind tabs (framing spec `2026-06-15-ux-sky-atlas-framing-design.md:428`).
3. **Readout strip**, 32 px, in mono: `5.0 x 2.3 deg . 6 panels . 00h42m44s +41 16'`. The design wrote 3.3, a 3x3's height; the 3x2 of 2.0 x 1.33 deg at 25% overlap is 5.0 x 2.3 deg (S4, #339).
4. **Scroller** with 44 px rows and six sections: WHERE, GRID, ANGLE, PANELS, RUN, CENTRING.

**Phone landscape (667-932 px wide)**: sky on the left at 55%, scroller on the right. This is chosen by an aspect or container query, never `lg:`, which strands phone landscape.

**Tablet and desktop**: a new Overlay `full` variant. `Overlay.tsx:80-84` has no full-screen variant today, and sizes still go through `--ov-*` variables. The sky flexes on the left beside a 360 px control column.

**As built** (S4, #189). The Overlay's `full` variant is built. `overlayGeometry("full", ...)` answers the same at every width, an `absolute inset-0 flex` wrap around an `overlay-full sheet-enter` surface, with no `--ov-*` variables of its own, because an inline one would outrank the stylesheet: the size is one rule in `index.css`, `.overlay-surface.overlay-full`, which sets all four variables to 100% (not `100vw`, which counts a desktop scrollbar, and not a `dvh` value, which in a browser without `dvh` makes the `@supports` fallback invalid, #354), pads the top and side safe-area insets because `index.html` sets `viewport-fit=cover`, and draws no border.
