The four older variants are unchanged. The readout strip's text is `readoutStrip` in `framingModel.ts`, the modal's pure model: `5.0 x 2.3 deg · 6 panels · 00h42m44s +41 16'` for the reference 3x2. The first wave built no sheet to lay out; the second built it (2.1), in the `full` variant at every width.

**As built, the layout** (S4, #189). The layout is one stylesheet, `framing.css`, and the arrangement is chosen by container queries on the sheet itself (`.tfs` is a size container named `tfs`), never by a width breakpoint. Phone portrait is the default: a sticky 48 px header (CANCEL, `FRAME <name>`, DONE, a HonestButton), the sky pinned outside the scroller at `min(100vw, 52svh)` and at `40svh` while a text field has focus (`data-typing`), each with a `vh` line before it for a browser without `svh`, a 32 px mono readout strip, and the scroller, the only part that scrolls, of 44 px rows. Phone landscape, a sheet wider than tall and no taller than 540 px (`@container tfs (min-aspect-ratio: 1/1) and (max-height: 540px)`), puts the sky on the left at 55%; a sheet at least 720 px wide and taller than 540 px puts the sky beside a 360 px control column; and a browser without container queries reads the same two arrangements off the viewport, which the sheet fills. The sky is the largest square it holds, and inside the sheet SkyCanvas's 320 px floor is lifted (`.tfs-sky-fit [role="application"]`), which on a phone in landscape pushed the canvas's bottom edge under the sky's clip. MOVE SKY and MOVE GRID are stacked, since side by side they covered the compass's N in a 312 px sky, and they sit in the sky's top-right corner, where SkyCanvas keeps room for overlay controls (`.tfs-move`). The second wave put them top-left, under SkyCanvas's touch chip, and on a phone the chip took the tap meant for MOVE SKY, which the real-page probe found (S4, #384).

**As built since S5** (S5, #354). The other four variants no longer put a `dvh` value in any variable either. `overlayGeometry` gives each a bare fraction of the viewport's height, `--ov-max-h-frac`: 0.85 for a sheet, 0.6 for a dock below `lg`, 0.8 for a corner and 0.7 on a phone, and for a center 0.92 on a phone and 1 above it, less a `--ov-max-h-gap` of 2rem, the wrapper's padding. `index.css` multiplies the fraction by `100dvh` in its main rule and by `100vh` in the `@supports not (height: 100dvh)` fallback, so the fallback clamps every variant. A caller's own `--ov-max-h` still replaces the fraction whole, so `full`'s 100% and every caller's cap compute as before wherever `dvh` exists; until S7 six callers handed a `dvh` value to `--ov-max-h` and lost the clamp without it (#417). Since S7 no caller sets `--ov-max-h` itself (S7, #417): each hands its fraction in `--ov-max-h-frac`, the centre callers with `--ov-max-h-gap` 0px since their fraction already leaves the room (`FlowWizard.tsx`, `QuickFlow.tsx` and `TonightPanel.tsx` at 0.9, as S6's `SendToWizardSheet.tsx` already did, and `ClassicSkyTools.tsx` at 0.92), and the two sheets their own (`FlowEditSheet.tsx` 0.76, `FlowPaletteSheet.tsx` 0.72), so every one keeps the clamp without `dvh`. The Overlay's own comment and `index.css`'s said six still do until the S7 integration corrected them.

### 2.3 The sky and the panel layer

- **One `SkyCanvas`**, extended only through props and never forked (`ui/src/components/atlas/SkyCanvas.tsx:95-153`). It is mounted on a **local** FramingSession seeded from the node's params, following the CompassSurvey precedent (`ui/src/components/sky/ClassicAtlasSky.tsx:130-147`). It never touches the global `store.framing` (`ui/src/store.ts:1283-1318`). That singleton is how one target's mosaic appeared on another target's flow in review #3 (`flowLane.ts:80-86`).
- **Survey and interaction.** Survey tiles come from the existing HiPS engine and the offline pack. The compass, RotateHandle and the live PointingFrame are unchanged. The live footprint is drawn only from telemetry and is never fed the planned target (`atlasFov.ts:8-15`).
- **New `panels` prop, the PanelLayer.** It draws each panel from the **server's** panel coordinates through `skyToView` (`atlasFov.ts:151`) and `fovCornersSky` (`:196`), with HTML labels (`1-1`) and the run-order number.
  - Labels must never come from `FovOverlay`'s screen-space grid, which is a point reflection of the server layout (`FovOverlay.tsx:98-122`), or from `panelRects`, which mirrors columns (`mosaic.ts:228-250`).
  - While the operator drags, the client mirror (`lib/framing.ts:216`) previews the grid. On every settle the modal calls `POST /api/framing/mosaic` (`framing.py:317-347`) and redraws the labels from its answer.
- **Label convention.** 1-based `row-col` in the server convention: row 1 is the north edge and col 1 is the west edge at angle 0. That is the same as the Plan names `<name> r-c`. Server panel (0,0) is the north-west corner (`framing.py:176-231`), so on the north-up, east-left chart `1-1` sits top right.
- **Panel states are told apart by shape, not hue** (night mode):

  | State | Drawing |
  |---|---|
  | pending | thin stroke |
  | skipped | dashed, with an X |
  | shooting (run mode) | thick stroke plus corner ticks: the active-panel style the framing spec reserved, finally fed |
  | done | diagonal hatch |
  | set aside tonight | dotted, with "!" |

- **Gestures.** One-finger drag moves the sky under the pinned grid, which moves the mosaic centre (the existing `panTo`, `SkyCanvas.tsx:457`). A **MOVE GRID / MOVE SKY** toggle switches to dragging the grid over a still sky. That needs a new additive `frameCenter` prop, projected through `skyToView`. Pinch zooms 0.1 to 10 deg. Tapping a panel toggles skip.

**As built** (S4, #189). The PanelLayer is built, and SkyCanvas takes it through props and is not forked. `PanelLayer.tsx` places each panel from the server's panel coordinates, its centre through `skyToView` and its outline through `fovCornersSky` at the panel's own `rotation_deg`, or the canvas's angle when it carries none (`placePanels`), hit-tests a tap inside the outlines, the nearest centre winning in an overlap (`panelAt`), and labels each `row-col` from 1 (`panelLabel`), so server panel (0, 0) reads `1-1` at the top right of the north-up, east-left chart. The five states are the table's, drawn in one ink (`PANEL_STATES`, `PanelShapes`, `PanelLabels`): pending a thin stroke, skipped dashed with an X, shooting a thick stroke with corner ticks, done a diagonal hatch, set aside dotted with "!" in its label. The ticks point outward, so they cannot be read as the live PointingFrame's inward brackets. SkyCanvas's new props are all optional, and with none it renders as before: `panels`, `panelFov`, `frameCenter`, `onPanelTap`, which hands back the server's 0-based row and col, and `onFrameCenterChange`, added so that MOVE GRID has a way to report where the grid was dragged. A non-empty `panels` replaces FovOverlay's screen-space grid, and a tap goes to an object marker first, then to a panel, then clears the pick. `tests/fixtures/mosaic_panels_3x2.json` is a real `POST /api/framing/mosaic` answer, which `test_framing_panels_fixture.py` grades against the server and the canvas tests read. The first wave did not build the local FramingSession, the modal that mounts the canvas (2.1) or the MOVE GRID / MOVE SKY toggle; the second built all three (below). Until S5 object labels did not make room for panel labels (below).

**As built, the modal's sky** (S4, #189). `FramingSky.tsx` mounts the one SkyCanvas on the sheet's local session: the draft of the node's params (`draftFromParams`), the zoom and the survey are the sheet's own state, and neither file reads or writes `store.framing`. Its panels are the route's answer for the current spec once the draft has settled for `framingTiming.settleMs` (400 ms), and the client mirror's (`mosaicGrid`) until then; an answer is kept only while its request is still the one the draft makes, so a slow answer never draws a layout it was not asked about. MOVE SKY drags the sky under the pinned grid, which moves the mosaic centre; MOVE GRID holds the sky still and drags the grid (`onFrameCenterChange`), and there the arrow keys move the view alone. A tap on a panel toggles its skip, pinch zooms from `ZOOM_MIN_DEG` (0.1 deg) to `ZOOM_MAX_DEG` (10 deg), and the rotate handle turns the angle; turned on a block at ANY ANGLE, it sets ROTATE TO, or CAMERA FIXED AT when the rig says it has no rotator. While an RA or Dec is half typed the sky stays on the last centre that could be read, so the canvas is not remounted on every keystroke.

**As built since S5** (S5, #385, #404). Each drawn panel label's box is among the boxes an object label keeps out of (`reservedBoxes`, over `panelLabelBoxes`, each label measured by `textWidth`), so an object's name no longer covers the label a tap on the panel toggles. In panel mode the "Your camera" label is not drawn (`showCamLabel`): wherever it went it would state a size no outline has, since panels are outlined at the field they were tiled for, which can differ from the live camera's, and the GRID section states both. Until S7 the "Object size" legend sat on the middle row's west-most panel label on a grid with an odd number of rows (#425). The modal's sky keeps a degraded state of its own and wires SkyCanvas's three survey signals (`surveyDegraded`, `onSurveyError`, `onSurveyLoad`), so on a rig with no survey source it shows SkyCanvas's UX-07 empty state where it read LOADING for good; SkyCanvas's image path, which the modal takes without WebGL or a survey slug, is gated on the same signal. Until S7 that fallback copy said "enable online fetch" when online fetch was already on (#426). In run mode the panels are drawn from the live run (2.6).

**As built since S7** (S7, #425, #426, #440). In panel mode the "Object size" legend looks for a place on the canvas that meets none of the reserved boxes, the panel labels among them, rather than pinning itself beside the object's ellipsis (`LEGEND_TEXT`, `reservedBoxes`), so it no longer covers a label a tap toggles; outside panel mode it is placed as before. The degraded line is worded by what the canvas draws (`degradedLine`): the tile engine's "Survey tiles not loading. Retrying automatically.", and otherwise the last image or the schematic it shows, and the empty state asks for online fetch only when it is off (`NO_SURVEY_OFFLINE`), saying the survey is not arriving when it is on (`NO_SURVEY_ONLINE`). The modal's square is sized from the height its column leaves, not from a fixed box: SkyCanvas wraps the square in `.sky-canvas-square`, and `framing.css` makes the fit box and the square `min(100cqw, 100cqh)` of their container, so the lines under the canvas no longer overflow it and clip the MOVE SKY toggle, nor, on a phone, cut the canvas and the degraded line (#440, #465). The compass sky asks for a survey that is served (#494). The object labels' room for the panel labels, which the S5 review took for unbuilt, is S5's, and a mutant shows it held (noted on #385). Open: the empty state's sentence is still drawn across the grid and its panel labels, and object labels do not keep off the legend (#491); until H4 the first tap on a WHERE result was lost on a phone (#492), and a view of fewer than 8 tiles said LOADING for 45 s or more (#493), both fixed in H4 (below).

**As built since H4** (H4, #492, #493). A WHERE result takes one tap: the result buttons cancel their mousedown, so the field keeps its focus through the press and nothing under the finger blurs or moves, and the pick then blurs the field, which closes a phone's keyboard and the modal's typing mode as before (`CatalogSearch.tsx`; `framingCatalogPick.test.tsx`). Keeping typing mode through a focus move inside the widget would not have held in WebKit or in Firefox on macOS, where a pressed button takes no focus. The tile engine calls a view source-less once `allFailingAt(need)` of its fetches have failed in a row, `need` the length of the frame's fetch plan, the visible tiles and the coarser tiles that could still be drawn into them, held between 1 and 8, so a view of fewer than 8 tiles no longer waits out the retry (`TileEngine.tsx`; `tileEngineAllFailing.test.tsx`); and FramingSky keeps that verdict in module memory keyed by survey, not in its own state, so a remount, as on a phone's turn, keeps it, and the engine's first drawn frame clears it (`framingSkyDegraded.test.tsx`). #491 is still open.

### 2.4 Sections

**WHERE**

- CatalogSearch, reused from `QuickFlow.tsx:346-351`. RA and Dec in J2000 sexagesimal, parsed with `raHms`/`decDms` (`QuickFlow.tsx:54-90`).
- FIT OBJECT.
- SUGGEST GRID (ATL-05): `cols = ceil((size - fov*ov) / (fov*(1-ov)))`. It is a hint only until the catalogue carries major axis, minor axis and PA. Today it has `size_arcmin` only (`types.ts:938`).

**GRID**

- COLS and ROWS steppers, 1 to 10. OVERLAP from 0 to 50% in steps of 5, with quick chips at 15, 25 and 35. The default is 25%. It is **one server constant**, exported by `framing.py` and replacing both the 0.25 in `store.ts:1310` and the 0.15 in `next/hubs/sky/frame/mosaic.ts:36`. As built (H4, #461, recording what S6 and S7 did): the constant is `catalog/framing.py`'s `DEFAULT_OVERLAP`, 0.25, mirrored as `lib/framing.ts`'s `DEFAULT_OVERLAP` and held to it by `test_overlap_constant_one.py`. S6 made the store's framing seed (`store.ts`, where the 0.25 was) and the Sky's FRAME mode (`mosaic.ts`, whose 0.15 is gone and which re-exports the constant as `OVERLAP`) read it, and S7 derived the TARGET node's `overlap` default from it on both sides, in percent (`target_overlap_pct`, `nodeDefs.ts`), where each wrote a number of its own (3.1).
- Camera field: `Tiled for 2.00 x 1.33 deg at bin 1 (profile Refractor, matched 2026-09-23)`.
- **MATCH CAMERA** snapshots `effectiveOptics` (`lib/effective.ts`, profile-aware, #129) into `fovX`/`fovY` at bin 1.
- A banner appears when the live optics differ from the snapshot by more than 2%. When the live field would leave gaps, it states M5's sentence.
- The grid controls lock with a reason when optics are unset: "set the camera and focal length in Settings > Optics".

**ANGLE**

Three segments:

- **ANY ANGLE** writes `rotation = -1`. It is locked for grids larger than 1x1, with the reason "a grid is laid out at one camera angle".
- **ROTATE TO** commands the rotator. It is locked with a reason when the active profile has no rotator.
- **CAMERA FIXED AT** never commands the rotator. The run checks the angle instead (section 5.6).

Controls and readouts:

- A continuous degree field with nudges of plus or minus 1 and 15, the rotate handle, and the `[` `]` keys. There is no 15-degree snap. The existing dial shows 0 for other angles (`FramingCard.tsx:124`), which is issue I-24.
- A **USE MEASURED** chip, read from `status.sky_angle` (`hub.py:7001`, which nothing in `ui/src` consumes today): `camera measured 37.2 deg, 14 min ago, by the centring solve, pier west`. One tap lays the grid out at the angle the camera actually sits at. That is the no-rotator workflow, and nobody has to turn anything.
- The convention line: "degrees N through W, clockwise on this chart, as the plate solver reports (CROTA2); the rotator's sense is under test (#145)".
- The tolerance line, computed from the geometry (Appendix A.2): "this layout tolerates a camera error of 6.0 deg (convergence and angle error together use at most half the overlap at a four-panel corner)".

**PANELS**

- One row per panel in run order: order number, `r-c`, an on/off toggle, a progress bar (banked/owed from the progress route), and the set-aside reason when there is one.
- The peak-altitude column and the `MosaicNightCard` (`next/hubs/sky/frame/MosaicNightCard.tsx:49`, props-only) render only with `CAP_VIEW_SITE_DERIVED`. They are **hidden**, never shown empty, for a viewer.
- An ORDER select: Least complete first / Setting first / Grid order.

**RUN** (shown only when the block owns a stage; every number comes from the server compile, never computed in the client)

- A **Rotate panels every pass** toggle. It adds or removes the loop wire, which keeps one source of truth.
- Stay on a panel: `passes` (1 to 20) and "at least N minutes" (0 to 180).
- Counts: no control. Every new block counts accepted subs only (D8). A block that still counts every sub, because its flow predates the ruling, shows the one-line notice of Revision 2, ruling 2 instead.
- While a mosaic waits (for every mosaic in this flow): Shoot later targets, then come back / Wait for the mosaic. This row edits the flow's `whenWaiting` setting (1.6).
- Readouts, for the shipped default cycle (L R G B at 60 s, Ha OIII SII at 180 s, 45 cycles) on a 3x2:
  - `6 panels x 7 filters x 45 = 1890 subs`
  - `9.75 h per panel, 58.5 h in all`
  - `270 visits at 1 pass per visit`
  - `hop: not measured on this rig yet`, or once it has been measured, `hop 2 m 40 s, measured over 6 hops`. The efficiency figure appears only with a measured hop.
  - `meridian: no idle before the flip` for this 3x2 at Dec 41 (the pre-flip idle of 5.7, computed from the RA span of the panel centres, the plan's flip lead and the hop; none of these is site data). A compact 2x2 of 0.9 x 0.6 deg reads `meridian: up to 10.6 min idle before the flip`.
  - `focus: refocus on temperature` when the focuser temperature delta is armed, or `focus: a sweep only at the first panel; set a temperature delta to refocus as the night cools` when neither it nor `autofocus_every` is set (5.6).
- With `CAP_VIEW_SITE_DERIVED`: "this is a campaign: about 7.8 nights of 7.5 h before hops. The session stays armed and resumes at the next dusk." The old advice "Set DUSK to repeat nightly" is dropped because it changes nothing: `engine.start` arms auto-resume on every run (`engine.py:795`). DUSK's "Single night" is replaced by the option "Automatic resume on subsequent nights until capture quota is fulfilled", default ON (Revision 2, ruling 7, #195). When it is off, this line reads "this is a campaign of about 7.8 nights, and automatic resume is off for this flow: continue it by hand each night".

**CENTRING**

- Tolerance in arcmin (default 1.2) and tries (default 3). These equal what actually runs today: 0.02 deg and 3 (`hub.py:6387-6391`).
- "If a panel will not centre or reach its angle": Auto / Skip it this pass / Shoot anyway. Auto means skip for a mosaic panel and shoot for a single target.

**As built** (S4, #189). RUN's numbers come from the compile: `_compile_payload` answers two keys beside `plan`, `structural`, `issues` and `unmapped`, computed in `flows/readouts.py` from the SequencePlan the preview compiled and never from the node's params. `readouts` is `{node_id: block}` for every TARGET block the plan shoots, each block holding `mode` (`rotate`, `sequential` or `single`), `panels`, `steps`, `rounds`, `subs_per_panel`, `subs_total`, `pass_s`, `panel_s`, `total_s`, `passes`, `visit_min_s`, `visit_passes` (5.3's `max(passes, ceil(minVisit / pass))`, a function the module exports for Tonight), `visits_per_panel`, `visits_total`, `hop_s`, `hop_measured`, `efficiency` (absent, not null, until a hop has been measured), `preflip_idle_s` (5.7's cost 1 with cut visits, with A.4's assumed 10 s of per-frame overhead, `FRAME_OVERHEAD_S`), `angle_tolerance_deg`, and `focus` (`temperature`, `frames` or `once`) with `autofocus_every` and `refocus_delta_c`. A single target's group fields are null, its hop included, so a flow with no mosaic reads the same whatever the rig knows; a pool, a dropped block and a block whose every panel is skipped have no entry, and the key is `{}` when the compile refused. `rig` is `{fov_deg, fov_from, has_rotator, hop_s, hop_samples, hop_measured}` (`rig_readout`), its hop the measured mean or null, never the seed. Neither carries site data. For the shipped default cycle on the 3x2 the block reads the numbers above: 1890 subs, 35100 s per panel and 210600 s in all, 270 visits at one pass per visit, and no idle before the flip at Dec 41. `tests/fixtures/flow_readouts_m31.json` is the answer for the eighth Example, and the modal's pure model mirrors the block field for field (`RunReadouts` in `framingModel.ts`, spelt by `runLines`); the first wave had no editor to print it, and the second's RUN section prints it (below). ANGLE's dial is fixed (S4, #173): the #/next framing card hands its dial the angle itself, an angle that is not a 15-degree stop gets a stop of its own, and a PA field with nudges of 1 and 15 degrees steps from where the frame is (`FramingCard.tsx`: `rotationStops`, `wrapDeg`, `degLabel`). The counts notice of Revision 2 ruling 2 is built as data (S4 orchestrator ruling 8): the slice keeps the read's note as `countsNote` until the flow is closed, `countsNotice` in `countsNotice.ts` decides the line from the graph and that note, and a save that switched the counts writes the switch into the graph on screen (`acceptCounts`), so the line comes down. The first wave drew it nowhere; the second drew it in the RUN section and on the phone stage list.

**As built, the sections** (S4, #189). The second wave built the six sections, one file each under `framing/sections/`, in the order above, every row 44 px and every button that cannot act one that says why when pressed (a HonestButton). WHERE (`WhereSection.tsx`): the CatalogSearch QuickFlow uses, which fills the name and both coordinates at once, since `to_plan` reads the coordinates and never the name (#190); NAME, RA and DEC fields; FIT OBJECT; and SUGGEST GRID (`suggestGrid`), which says it is a hint because the catalogue holds one size and no axes. GRID (`GridSection.tsx`): COLS and ROWS steppers from 1 to `GRID_MAX` (10), OVERLAP from 0 to `OVERLAP_MAX` (50%) in steps of `OVERLAP_STEP` (5) with the quick chips `OVERLAP_CHIPS` (15, 25 and 35), the camera-field line, MATCH CAMERA, which snapshots the live field at bin 1 and its provenance (`matchCamera`), and the drift banner; the grid controls lock with "set the camera and focal length in Settings > Optics" (`NO_OPTICS`) only when there is neither a snapshotted field nor a live one (`gridLock`), and until S5 a draft that became a grid at ANY ANGLE was set to ROTATE TO, or to CAMERA FIXED AT on a rig with no rotator (below). ANGLE (`AngleSection.tsx`): the three segments, ANY ANGLE locked on a grid with "a grid is laid out at one camera angle" (`ANY_ANGLE_ON_A_GRID`) and ROTATE TO locked only when the rig says it has no rotator (`NO_ROTATOR`; unknown is not no, `angleLocks`); the degree field with nudges of `NUDGES` (-15, -1, 1, 15), no snap; USE MEASURED from `status.sky_angle` (`useMeasured`); the convention line; and the tolerance line from the compile's `angle_tolerance_deg`. PANELS (`PanelsSection.tsx`): a row per panel in run order with its label, its toggle and a progress bar from the progress route when its grid is the draft's, the skipped panels last; least complete first orders by the fraction banked and then snake order, ResumeArm's rule, since the progress route carries no visit times; setting first is listed in grid order, with the note "setting first is ordered at the run, from the site: listed here in grid order" (`SETTING_FIRST_NOTE`); the altitude column and the `MosaicNightCard` render only for a holder of `view.site_derived` and are absent, not empty, for anyone else. RUN (`RunSection.tsx`), shown only when the block owns a stage: "Rotate panels every pass" (`ROTATE_LABEL`), the loop wire, locked with a reason when the block has one panel or its lane has no single tail with a "pass done"; `passes` and "at least" minutes; "While a mosaic waits (for every mosaic in this flow)" (`WHEN_WAITING_LABEL`), the flow's `whenWaiting`; the counts line (`countsNotice`) while the block counts every sub taken; the readout lines (`runLines`); and the campaign line, the block's `total_s` over the night Tonight answers, shown only for a holder of `view.site_derived` and only when it is more than one night (`campaignLine`). RUN's numbers for an edited layout come from a draft compile of the framed graph (`framingApi.compileDraft`) once it has settled, never from the store's `flows.compiled`, and a principal without `control.capture`, which that compile needs, is told so rather than sent (`COMPILE_NEEDS_ACCESS`). CENTRING (`CentringSection.tsx`): tolerance, tries and "If a panel will not centre or reach its angle".

**As built, the counts line in the editors** (S4 orchestrator ruling 8, #189). Since the third wave both editors draw the line too, for as long as `countsNotice` answers one, with no dismiss: the classic editor above every tier's body, as a status line (`FlowCountsLine` in `FlowEditor.tsx`), and the #/next canvas directly under its toolbar, the row that holds SAVE (`FlowCanvasToolbar.tsx`). Each reads the line from the graph on every store write, as the phone stage list does, so the save that writes `acceptCounts` into the graph takes it down without the flow being reopened. The classic line stands while the flow runs too, when rejected subs are being counted: `FlowCountsLine` reads nothing of the run, and a case has held that since a second verification of the third wave's tasks (S4, #401).

**As built since S5, an angle for a grid** (S5 orchestrator ruling 1, #411). A draft at ANY ANGLE that becomes a grid, by a stepper or SUGGEST GRID, stays at ANY ANGLE: S4 turned it into ROTATE TO at PA 0, an angle nobody chose, which a DONE then sent to the rotator from a section below GRID, off screen on a phone. DONE is locked until an angle is chosen, with "a grid is laid out at one camera angle: choose one, or USE MEASURED after a plate solve" (`NO_ANGLE_ON_A_GRID`, from `gridAngleLock`), for a set mode whose rotation reads as none too, which M2 refuses. The readout strip gains a line with the angle the grid is laid out at (`stripAngle`: "rotate to 30.0 deg", "camera fixed at 30.0 deg", "any angle" or "no angle"). With a measurement in `status.sky_angle` the sheet offers the measured angle, beside that line and in ANGLE's USE MEASURED row (`angleOffer`), and writes it only when pressed (`takeOffer`): as CAMERA FIXED AT when the rig says it has no rotator or the operator already chose CAMERA FIXED AT, and as ROTATE TO otherwise, since an unknown rotator is not no rotator. The offer is left out while the sheet is frozen. A press on a segment and the rotate handle set an angle as before, one the operator chose, and a 1x1 at ANY ANGLE owes none, its plain USE MEASURED still setting CAMERA FIXED AT, the no-rotator workflow above.

**As built since S5, the sections** (S5, #413, #412, #408, #339). On one panel RUN leaves out the rotate toggle, `passes` and "at least" rather than lock them (`onePanel`), and while no TARGET in the flow has more than one panel it says what the classic overview says, that the setting changes nothing yet (`noMosaic`, `WHEN_WAITING_NO_MOSAIC`). A single target's focus line reads "focus: a sweep only at the start; set a temperature delta to refocus as the night cools", since it has no first panel; a mosaic's still says "at the first panel". CENTRING's question is labelled from the vocabulary's field (`IF_NOT_CENTRED_LABEL`). PANELS says how grid order is listed (`GRID_ORDER_NOTE`), as it says how setting first is. The model takes the measured angle's shape from `types.ts` (`MeasuredAngle`, four keys of `SkyAngleRecord` until S7, five since, below), where it kept a copy. Both night cards write the grid columns by rows, `ACROSS THE 3×2 MOSAIC` (`MosaicNightCard.tsx`, and the classic Atlas's `MosaicNight.tsx`).

**As built since S7, the measured angle and the modal's numbers** (S7, #439, #358). `MeasuredAngle` takes a fifth key of `SkyAngleRecord`, `exposed_at`, and USE MEASURED's age is the measured frame's, not the solve's (`measuredAt`: `exposed_at` when it is a finite number, else `solved_at`), so an angle solved late off a saved frame no longer reads as fresh. The modal's own `coerceParam` keeps a number only when it is finite, as the slice's does, so "Infinity" or "1e999" typed into a numeric field reads as the field's default in the modal as in the store, and the RUN section's draft compile no longer sends a null for it (`coerceParamFinite.test.ts`).

### 2.5 DONE

- DONE stays locked, with the reason "waiting for the server's panel positions", until `POST /api/framing/mosaic` has answered for the current spec.
- Offline, or as a viewer (that route needs `CAP_VIEW_SITE_DERIVED`), DONE unlocks with the chip "panels from the offline mirror; the run computes them on the server". That is true, because `to_plan` calls the same `compute_mosaic`.
- DONE commits **one** new slice action, `flowsApplyFraming(id, patch, loop)`. It writes every changed param and adds or removes the loop wire in a single slice write, with one dirty/compile cycle. `flowsSetParam` writes one key per call today (`flowsSlice.ts:264-279`). Coercion follows each default's type, as `flowsSetParam` does.
- The card turns valid only after the compiler answers.
- **Re-frame carry or restart** (Revision 2, ruling 3). The readout strip shows how far the new layout moves against the block's anchor (3.3), live: `moved 4.2' of the 10.0' this grid allows: counts carry over`. Only when the move reaches the threshold, while the progress route reports banked subs, does DONE ask first: "Re-framing moves the panels 14.8', more than the 10.0' this grid allows, so all 6 panels start from zero: 212 banked subs belong to the old layout and stay on disk." Changing rows or cols always restarts, with the same question. The numbers come from the server (`POST /api/framing/mosaic` with the anchor, 3.3). Offline, the mirror computes them and the chip says "the server decides at save".
- Skip toggles never ask, because skip is not part of the identity. Re-enabling a panel restores its progress. CONTINUE keeps this promise: a skipped panel's banked steps are exempt from the dropped-steps refusal (5.9).

**As built** (S4, #189). The slice's half of DONE is built: `flowsApplyFraming(id, patch, loop?)` in `flowsSlice.ts` writes every changed param, coerced by its default's type through `coerceParam` (which `flowsSetParam` now shares), and sets the loop wire as `loop` asks through `withLoop` (1.4), judged on the patched block, in one graph write, one dirty flip and one compile, and it resolves when that compile answers; a DONE that changes nothing, or names a node that is gone, writes and compiles nothing. `flowsAddNode` returns the new id. The DONE logic is in the modal's pure model, `framingModel.ts`: `doneState` keeps DONE locked with the reason "waiting for the server's panel positions" (`WAITING_FOR_PANELS`) until `POST /api/framing/mosaic` has answered for the current spec, opens it with the chip "panels from the offline mirror; the run computes them on the server" (`OFFLINE_MIRROR`) when the route cannot be asked or its answer failed, and leaves it open with no chip when there is nothing to ask about yet (no typed coordinates, no field, or a grid past 10). Offline, no move is computed before DONE: there is no client copy of `reframe_carry`'s corner geometry, so the move line reads "the server decides at save" (`SERVER_DECIDES`), only a change of rows or cols asks (`reframeDecision`), and a block with no stored anchor gets no reframe. Online, MATCH CAMERA on a block anchored with no camera field is judged as the save will judge it (S4 orchestrator ruling 4): the route's `_reframe_answer` completes such an anchor before it measures (3.3), so the modal does not ask about a restart the save will not make. A save that re-anchors is announced by one warning toast naming its blocks (`reanchorToast`, S4 orchestrator ruling 8), and the flow log says for each what changed, from its row's `reason` (3.3). The first wave built no sheet, DONE button or question; the second built them (below).

**As built, DONE on the sheet** (S4, #189). DONE is a HonestButton in the header, locked by `doneState`, or while its write is in flight with "writing the framing and waiting for the compiler" (`CHECKING`). A press asks first when `reframeDecision` says the save would restart banked counts, with RE-FRAME and KEEP EDITING, and the question goes as soon as the draft moves or DONE's lock changes, so RE-FRAME commits only the layout it asked about (#377). The write is one `flowsApplyFraming(id, patch, loop)`, where `patch` (`framingPatch`) holds every draft param whose coerced value differs from the stored one and `loop` is left out unless RUN's toggle was touched, except that a block becoming a mosaic gets the loop (1.4, "when the wire is added") and a looped mosaic becoming one panel has its wire lifted. When the flow's "While a mosaic waits" row changed, `flowsSetSetting` writes it first, which compiles nothing, so the framing write's one compile covers it; a DONE that changed only that row runs one compile itself, since only the compile copies the setting into each block's `mosaic.when_waiting`. The sheet closes when that compile has answered. CANCEL writes nothing, the setting included. While DONE's write waits for its compile, the sheet takes no way out and no edit (S4, #382): the write has happened by then, so CANCEL can no longer mean "write nothing", and a close taken in the wait was followed by DONE's own when the compile answered, which ran the host's `onClose` twice. CANCEL is a HonestButton too, locked with `CHECKING`, and Escape and the scrim go through the same refusal (`requestClose`), so each says why and closes nothing. The draft is frozen over the same wait (`frozen`: the fieldset disabled and the sky read-only, as in view mode), because DONE wrote the draft as it stood when pressed and the sheet then closes: a name typed, a column added or a panel tapped in the wait was drawn, accepted and dropped with the sheet. DONE's own close runs only while the sheet is still mounted (`alive`, set again by a StrictMode remount), since the host can take the sheet down in the wait, as a browser Back does in #/next. The wait is bounded by the compile's request timeout, 15 s (`timeoutFor`), and `flowsCompile` settles either way, so the lock traps no one.

**As built since S5** (S5 orchestrator ruling 1, #411). DONE is locked first by an angle a grid still owes (`gridAngleLock`, 2.4), ahead of `doneState`'s lock and the write in flight, so a DONE never writes a grid at ANY ANGLE, or at a set mode with no angle, which M2 would refuse at the run.

### 2.6 Run mode

While this flow's session is active, the same sheet opens read-only, fed by `state.group` (section 5.10) and the progress route. The current panel gets corner ticks, panels fill as their subs land, and set-aside panels show their reason. What the operator framed is what they watch.

**As built in S4** (S4, #189). The sheet takes `viewOnly`, and with it opens read-only as a read-only Example does, with the reason line "This flow's session is running: its framing opens to view, not to edit." (`RUNNING_VIEW_ONLY`). S4 passed it from nowhere and fed the sheet no `state.group`: its panels showed pending, skipped and done, from the skip list and the progress route, and never shooting or set aside, or a set-aside reason.

**As built** (S5, #189). Both doors open the sheet with `viewOnly` while the flow's session runs, which one reader decides, `flowRunLive` in `flowRunState.ts`, from the progress route's session (since S7 the sessions the slice knows as the flow's, below) and the rig's sequence state: the classic editor through `FramingHostSheet` in `FlowEditor.tsx`, so the editor itself does not re-render when a run starts or ends, and #/next through `FlowFrameSheet.tsx`. The reason line says the session is running before it would say the flow is an Example, since a running flow can be one. In run mode the sheet finds the block's group in the sequence state by the progress block's `group_id`, never by name (`groupForBlock`), and each panel's live state in it (`runPanelsOf`, over `panelStateOf`), only while the progress block's grid is the draft's, since on another grid "2-2" is another piece of sky; outside run mode it reads no group. The sky draws each panel by `panelDrawState`: skipped first, then set aside, then shooting, which beats done because the progress count lags the run, then done and pending. PANELS draws a line under a panel the run is doing something to, "1-2: shooting now" or "2-1: set aside tonight: " and the engine's reason. Since W7 (WP-49, D-07, owner-approved 2026-09-30) a centring set-aside that may still expire tonight reads "2-1: set aside for now: " and the engine's reason, with a "; tried once more tonight" tail, which `run.forNow` decides; every other set-aside still reads "set aside tonight" as before. The group is read whenever the run is live, so the panel it names is drawn shooting while the run is paused, holding for cloud or aborting too (#451). The sheet shows none of the run's timing, no meridian countdown and no visit clock (5.10). A run that starts while an editable sheet holds unsaved edits freezes the sheet on that draft, and one that ends while it is open makes it editable again. Until S7, after every save made during a run, run mode let go for one progress round trip, because each progress read that is not live cleared the slice's progress first, and a progress read that failed never engaged it at all (#449).

**As built since S7** (S7, #449, #509, #528, #451). Both doors, the RUN button, the readouts and the slice's own live refresh ask `flowRunLive` of the sessions the slice knows as the open flow's (`knownSessions`, over `FlowsState.sessionIds`), where they asked it of the progress answer: a progress answer adds its session to the list, a run started from the editor adds its own, and a save, a failed read and a reopen of the same flow keep it, so run mode holds through a save made during the run and engages although a progress read fails; opening another flow replaces the list, and closing the editor clears it. PANELS prints `runRowText`, which leaves the panel's label out when the engine's set-aside reason already names that panel as a whole label, "set aside tonight: centring failed on 2-2 on 3 consecutive visits", where it said the panel twice (#509). In run mode a panel set aside tonight takes no run order (`panelRows`), so PANELS' number and the sky's order badge no longer name, as the run's next panel, the one the run has given up on (#528). `panelStateOf` answers a `current` state for the panel the group is on while the run is paused, holding or aborting, which PANELS words "current panel, run paused", "current panel, holding for cloud" and "current panel, run stopping", and the sheet's reader, `runPanelsOf`, hands it the sequence state, which both require since the S7 integration, so the modal no longer says "shooting now" through a pause, a hold or an Abort. The sky still draws that panel with the corner ticks (`panelDrawState`), since it is the panel the visit is on, and its label tells a screen reader PANELS' words (the panel's `words`) (#451, built by the S7 integration). Until H4 an Abort during a cloud hold also left the hold's reason standing in the aborting and aborted states (#513).

**As built since H4** (H4, #513, #534). An Abort or a SafetyAbort during a cloud hold takes the hold off the state it publishes: the aborting state, and the hold's own last publish before the terminal one, carry no hold and a sky that is not holding, by one rule for both (`_hold_cleared`; `test_h4_abort_clears_the_hold.py`), so the sheet's panel reads "current panel, run stopping" through an Abort, never "holding for cloud". A panel whose centring set-aside can still expire (5.1) is set aside for now, and the engine's line says "set aside for now, not for the night"; PANELS still words it "set aside tonight", because `types.ts`'s `SetAsideRecord` does not carry the record's kind and the published `group.set_aside` has no mark for it.

### 2.7 What is stored and what is not

**Stored**: node params only (section 3.1).

**Never stored**:

- Panel centres. `to_plan` derives them on every compile; `mosaic.ts:3-8` calls a client copy "a second truth about where the telescope points".
- Progress, which lives in the ledger.
- Altitudes, which are site-derived and fetched when needed.
- The survey image.

Survey choice and zoom are a per-viewer convenience in `localStorage`, keyed by flow and node id, with every access wrapped in try/catch.

**As built** (S4, #189). DONE stores node params only: `framingPatch` reads the draft's keys (`DRAFT_KEYS`), which leave out `counts`, which is not offered, and `frameAnchor`, which the server writes, so the modal can never write an anchor the server did not choose. The panels it draws are the route's answer or the mirror's, held in the sheet and never written. The per-viewer convenience is `loadViewPrefs` and `saveViewPrefs`, under the key `astrodeck-framing-view:<flow id>/<node id>` (`VIEW_PREFS_PREFIX`), each access in try/catch and answering the defaults when storage fails; a flow with no id yet is never stored, since node ids repeat across unsaved flows, and nor is a block whose flow or node id `encodeURIComponent` cannot spell (`viewPrefsKey` answers no key): a lone surrogate, which a flow file can hold, made it throw inside the sheet's first render and took the modal down (#386). The zoom is kept; there is no survey chooser yet, so the survey is the stored one or the default.

---

## 3. Data model and compile path

### 3.1 TARGET params (flat scalars, as `flowsTypes.ts:24-30` requires)

A NodeDef gains `create_params`. These are overrides applied when a node is **created**: by palette drop, by the wizard, or in an example fixture. Missing-key defaults (`default_params`, merged by `with_defaults`, `flows/models.py:67-88`) reproduce old behaviour. New blocks are created with the new choices written out explicitly. That fixes the "semantics flip needs a migration" class structurally, and a stale browser tab POSTing an old-shaped graph cannot flip it either. `nodeDefs.ts` mirrors `createParams`, and the parity test covers it.

**As built** (S3, #189). The Created-as column is `NodeDef.created_as`, and `create_params`, a property of `NodeDef` and a function of the same name in `flows/nodes.py`, overlays it on the missing-key defaults; `nodeDefs.ts` mirrors the two as `createdAs` and `createParams`. `angle` has no missing-key default: `target_angle` derives it from `rotation` for a stored block with no `angle` key, and `targetAngle` in the mirror derives it the same way. The table below is that column, value for value. Its `name`, `ra` and `dec` rows give M31's name and coordinates as the missing-key defaults, which S3 kept where the design wrote `""`, and which stay M31's by S4 orchestrator ruling 7 (owner list item 37; #190): a missing key keeps its old meaning, so a TARGET saved without those keys still slews to M31, and every new block gets `""` through `create_params`, so a new block names no object until the operator gives it one.

| Key | Control | Missing-key default (old meaning) | Created as | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | text | `M31 - Andromeda` (kept by S4 orchestrator ruling 7) | `""` | display name and panel-name prefix |
| `ra`, `dec` | text | `00h 42m 44s`, `+41° 16′ 09″` (M31's; kept by S4 orchestrator ruling 7) | `""` | J2000 sexagesimal. Empty means "no usable coordinates" from `to_plan` (`to_plan.py:1062-1073`), so a new block, created blank, is never slewed to M31 unasked (#190) |
| `rotation` | number | `-1` (S0 changes it from 23.4) | `-1` | degrees in the CROTA2 convention. Negative means none; 0 is a real PA (`to_plan.py:1075-1091`) |
| `angle` | select | derived at compile: `rotation < 0` gives Any angle, otherwise Rotate to PA | `Any angle` | Any angle / Rotate to PA / Camera fixed at PA |
| `rows`, `cols` | number | `1` | `1` | 1 to 10 (`MosaicSpecIn`, `framing.py:52-83`) |
| `overlap` | number | `DEFAULT_OVERLAP` in percent, read each time the table is read (`target_overlap_pct`; derived since S7, recorded by H4, #461) | same | percent, 0 to 50 |
| `fovX`, `fovY` | number | `0` | `0` | bin-1 degrees snapshotted at framing. 0 means not framed |
| `fovFrom` | text | `""` | `""` | provenance line, for display only |
| `skip` | text | `""` | `""` | for example `3-1, 3-2` |
| `passes` | number | `1` | `1` | full filter passes per visit, 1 to 20 |
| `minVisit` | number | `0` | `0` | minutes, 0 to 180. Re-set in S7 from the measured hop cost |
| `order` | select | `Least complete first` | same | / Setting first / Grid order |
| `centerTol` | number | `1.2` | `1.2` | arcmin. Equals the hub default of 0.02 deg |
| `centerTries` | number | `3` | `3` | 1 to 5 |
| `ifNotCentred` | select | `Auto` | `Auto` | / Skip it this pass / Shoot anyway |
| `counts` | none (not offered; Revision 2, ruling 2) | `Every sub taken` (kept on load) | `Accepted subs` | sets `plan.count_mode`. The save path rewrites any other value to `Accepted subs` and says so |
| `frameAnchor` | none (server-written) | `""` (the current geometry is the anchor) | written at first save | canonical JSON of the geometry the counts started at; the identity key (3.3, Revision 2, ruling 3) |

`whenWaiting` is not a TARGET param. It is the flow-level setting `FlowGraph.settings.whenWaiting` (1.6, Revision 2, ruling 1). POOL gains the same `counts` treatment as TARGET (created `Accepted subs`, missing-key `Every sub taken`, switched at save), so a new pool-only flow is not the one new flow that counts rejects. DUSK WINDOW's `repeat` is replaced by `autoResume` (Revision 2, ruling 7).

FILTER CYCLE and CAPTURE LOOP gain the `pass` event output. TARGET gains `next`. No `FieldDef` control is added; `FieldDef` gains an optional `help` string for DUSK's info icon (ruling 7). The node-type count stays 21.

### 3.2 Compiled dict (`compile_plan`, still pure: no devices, no config, no clock)

A TARGET node still emits **one** entry, so the PLAN tab shows the compile verbatim and one block reads as one mosaic:

```
{"name", "ra", "dec", "rotation_deg", "node_id",
 "angle": "any" | "rotate" | "fixed",
 "mosaic": null | {"rows", "cols", "overlap", "fov_x", "fov_y", "fov_from",
                   "skip": [[r, c], ...], "order", "passes", "visit_min",
                   "require_centred", "when_waiting"},   # when_waiting copied from graph.settings
 "loop": bool,                       # a pass wire from an owned stage exists
 "centre": {"tol_arcmin", "attempts"},
 "count_mode": "accepted" | "attempts",
 "steps": [...]}                     # owned stages only (section 1.5)
```

`tonight.py` iterates compiled targets (`tonight.py:393-439`), so this shape gives Tonight one `compute_night` per block instead of per panel, with no extra work.

**As built** (S3, #189). `compile_plan` emits this entry (`_target_entry`), with two keys the block above leaves out. `follows` is on a TARGET or POOL entry that a multi-panel block's tail feeds, and names that block's node id (1.6); it is absent otherwise. `frame_anchor` is the block's stored `frameAnchor` (3.3), the last of `mosaic`'s thirteen keys, `rows`, `cols`, `overlap`, `fov_x`, `fov_y`, `fov_from`, `skip`, `order`, `passes`, `visit_min`, `require_centred`, `when_waiting` and `frame_anchor`, and it rides on the entry itself when `mosaic` is `null`, for a block of one panel. `overlap` stays a percent, `skip` holds 1-based `[row, col]` pairs in grid order (`parse_skip`, with a warn note for an entry that names no panel), and a centring or grid value that is not a number is `None`, which `to_plan` leaves to the hub's default or refuses. A POOL member's entry carries its pool's `count_mode`.

**As built** (S4, #339, #328). The skip note writes the grid's size in words, columns first (`grid_size`, S4 orchestrator ruling 1), and the panels row-column, as the labels are: "TARGET M31: skip '9-9' names no panel of this grid of 3 columns by 2 rows, so nothing is skipped for it. A panel is written row-column, from 1-1 to 2-3." `compile_plan` no longer raises on a graph the compile routes accept (#328). Every count it emits is read finite-only (`_finite`): a CAPTURE's `count`, a FILTER CYCLE's `cycles` and `perCycle`, a POOL's quota, DUSK FLATS' count and the calibration quota, so "inf" or "nan" reads as the count's default. `validation_errors` refuses a FILTER CYCLE's `cycles` or `perCycle`, or a POOL's `quota`, that is a number but not a finite one above 0 (`COUNT_PARAMS`), with "FILTER CYCLE 'c': 'cycles' is 'inf', and a count must be a finite number above 0" (`COUNT_REFUSAL`), so save and `/run` answer 422 and the compile routes list it under `structural`; text that is not a number is still read as the default. `flow_order` counts a wire as a step of the cursor only when it enters a flow input. What the routes still failed on past `compile_plan` was #362, which S5 fixed (below).

**As built since S5** (S5, #362). The compile routes and `/run` answer a graph they cannot run in words, never with a 500. `to_sequence_plan` turns a `ValidationError` from the plan's model, and one from the mosaic layout's, into `GraphNotRunnable`, naming the block, the field, the value and pydantic's bound (`_refused_values`): "TARGET M42: steps[0].exposure_s of 5000 cannot be used - input should be less than or equal to 3600." A typed position off the sphere is dropped, as `save_rules.current_anchor` drops it, and a finite RA outside 0 to 24 h is refused by name. Every number `compile_plan` emits is read finite-only (`_finite`), each with the default it had, and every text through `_text`; a DOME's shutter timeout is read through one helper for the node and the plan (`_shutter_timeout`). Tonight refuses in words a count that is no count, by validation's own predicate (`_not_a_count`), and answers a refused quota `None` (section 8's S3). `test_compile_route_never_500.py` holds the routes to all of it. Filed: a digit run past 4300 digits in a FILTER CYCLE slot or a skip still raised out of `compile_plan` and the doctor (#441), and a DUSK offset of "inf", which validation lets a save store, raised out of the brief (#423), both fixed in S7 (below).

**As built since S7** (S7, #441, #423). A digit run `int()` refuses reads as nothing: `nodes.parse_cycle_plan` drops the slot, its policy for an entry it cannot read, and `compile.parse_skip` lists the entry as unread, as it lists a row past the last, so neither the compile, the doctor nor Tonight raises on it; the regex is not narrowed, so "01-002" is still panel 1-2 (`test_s7_compile_digit_runs.py`, and `fixtures/skip_cases.json`, which the modal's mirror reads too). The brief reads a DUSK offset finite-only: a stored infinity, NaN or integer past a float's range is said as an offset that cannot be read as a number of minutes, so none is applied, which is what the compile and Tonight's window do (`_not_finite`). Every text param compiles by S7 orchestrator ruling 6's reading (3.3). Until H4 a fractional DUSK offset was stored, used by the compile and Tonight's window, cut toward zero by the brief and refused by `/run` in words naming a TARGET (#483). Open: the Flow Wizard refuses a slot row past 4300 digits in CPython's words, not the row's (#501).

**As built since H4** (H4, #483). A DUSK offset is whole minutes: the validation refuses a finite offset with a fractional part on every DUSK WINDOW on the canvas, text such as "-30.7" included, naming the card, its node id, the value and the whole minutes on either side, "DUSK WINDOW 'n1': 'offset' is -30.7, and the window opens a whole number of minutes from dusk: use -31 or -30" (`WHOLE_MINUTE_PARAMS`, `WHOLE_MINUTES_REFUSAL`); an infinity, a NaN or an integer past a float's range is not refused, since a save stores them (above). `to_plan` names a refused schedule field by the card that holds its value: the DUSK WINDOW for a key the DUSK WINDOW wrote (`_dusk_fields`, beside `_pool_overrides`), and a POOL member for its own (`test_h4_dusk_offset_whole_minutes.py`).

### 3.3 Expansion, identity and rig facts (`to_plan.to_sequence_plan`)

`to_sequence_plan(compiled, *, flow_id="", rig=...)`. Both `_compile_payload` (`app.py:4757-4812`) and `run_flow` (`app.py:5098`) pass `flow_id`.

**Expansion.** For an entry with `mosaic` set, `to_plan` does the following:

- Parse the coordinates the way it does today.
- Call `framing.compute_mosaic({ra_hours, dec_deg, rows, cols, overlap/100, rotation_deg, fov_x_deg, fov_y_deg})`. That is the one projection; there is no third copy.
- Drop the skipped panels.
- Emit one Target per remaining panel:
  - `name "<name> <row+1>-<col+1>"`, `panel_row`, `panel_col`
  - `mosaic_group = group_id`
  - `rotation_deg`: the PA when `angle == "rotate"`, otherwise `None`
  - `acquisition "cycle"` when any step is a cycle step or `loop` is true
  - `center_tolerance_arcmin`, `center_attempts`
  - `autofocus_skip_if_fresh = True`
  - schedule from the dusk block (`to_plan.py:471-491`)
  - steps expanded per panel through `_cycle_steps` (`to_plan.py:515-558`)
- Emit one `TargetGroup` (section 3.4). Its `skipped_ids` lists the deterministic target ids of the skipped panels, so CONTINUE can tell a skipped panel from a dropped one (5.9).
- **As built** (S3, #189). `_expand_mosaic` does this, in `compute_mosaic`'s order, each panel named `<name> <row>-<col>`, 1-based, with 0-based `panel_row` and `panel_col`. Its group carries `mode` `rotate` with the loop wire and `sequential` without, `visit_passes`, `visit_min_s`, the order, `require_centred` (false only for "Shoot anyway"), the layout angle as `pa_deg`, `rotate` for "Rotate to PA", `angle_tolerance_deg` from `framing.angle_tolerance_deg`, and `skipped_ids`. Its id is `identity.group_id` over `_block_key`, the block's anchor and grid, so a panel's id is a function of the flow, the block, the anchor and the panel, and `skip` is in none of them; without a flow id the group id is a fresh uuid4 and the panel ids are derived from it. A block whose every panel is skipped is dropped with a warn, never emitted as a group of no members. Its skip is kept (S4, #335): its panels' ids become the plan's own `skipped_ids` (3.4), which CONTINUE and the progress route read through `continuation.plan_skipped_ids` (5.9, 1.2). A grid number outside `TargetGroup`'s and `MosaicSpecIn`'s bounds (`GRID_MAX` and the rest), a centring value outside `Target`'s and a stored anchor that cannot be read are refused with `GraphNotRunnable`, and so are M1, M2, M12 and M13, every sentence at once (`_mosaic_refusals`, which reads `compile.lane_refusals`). The signature is `to_sequence_plan(compiled, graph=None, *, flow_id="", when=None, cool_to=None, camera_can_cool=False, closes_on_unsafe=False, rig=None)`, and `rig` is read for M5's loss only. A block of one panel stays one plain Target with its centring, commands its PA only for "Rotate to PA", and is keyed on its anchor with no grid, so one saved before S3 keys exactly as S1 keyed it.

**Identity.** `NS_FLOWS` is a fixed uuid constant in `to_plan`.

- `geometry_key` = the first 16 hex characters of sha256 over canonical JSON of `ra_hours` and `dec_deg` (to 1e-6), `rows`, `cols`, `overlap` (to 1e-4), `rotation_deg` (to 1e-3), and `fov_x`, `fov_y` (to 1e-5), taken from the block's **anchor**, not from its current geometry (Revision 2, ruling 3). **`skip` is excluded.** The compile entry carries the anchor as `mosaic.frame_anchor` (or on the entry for a 1x1 block); with no anchor (an unsaved preview) the current geometry is the anchor, so `compile_plan` stays pure.
- **The anchor rule.** `frameAnchor` is written by the server on every save (`_persist_flow`, so POST, PUT, the wizard and the quick flow alike), through one pure function in `catalog/framing.py`, `reframe_carry(anchor, new, *, at=None) -> {carry, max_move_deg, threshold_deg, reason}`:
  - no anchor yet (first save, or a flow saved before S3): the anchor becomes the current geometry.
  - **As built at the save** (S3, #189). The store's one writer applies the save rules, `FlowStore.save_and_report` through `save_rules.prepare_save`, so every door converges without `_persist_flow` doing it. `save_and_report` answers `(record, migrated, reanchored)`: `migrated` is `["counts"]` when the save switched a `counts` (3.1), and `reanchored` holds the rows named below. "No anchor yet" is read from the file the save replaces. With no prior node, or one that had no layout, the current geometry is the anchor. A prior node with no stored anchor had one implicitly, the geometry the compile keyed it on (for a block of one panel, S1's single-target shape), and the new geometry is measured against it with `reframe_carry`, so a flow saved before S3 and nudged in its first S3 save keeps its ids. A name-only block is placed through `tonight.resolve_target`, a block with no layout now keeps the anchor it had, and a stored anchor that is not one is treated as absent.
  - **As built at the routes** (S3, #189). `_persist_flow` writes through `save_and_report`, so POST, PUT, the wizard and the quick flow all answer `_save_answer`: the record as stored, `migrated` with one `{key, note}` per rule the save applied (`counts`, with `COUNTS_SWITCHED_NOTE`), an object rather than the bare `"counts"` so that an answer a client sends back is still a record `FlowRecord` reads, and `reanchored`, on every answer and empty when nothing restarted. `GET /api/flows/{flow_id}` adds ruling 2's second sentence (`COUNTS_DORMANT_ADDENDUM`) to the counts note when the session Run would continue is dormant.
  - `rows` or `cols` differ from the anchor's, or `rotation` crosses between "any angle" (negative) and a set angle: re-anchor. The counts restart.
  - otherwise lay out both geometries with `compute_mosaic`, place each panel's four corners at its angle, and take `max_move_deg`, the largest angular distance any corner moves. The corners, not the centres, make one number cover a shift, a turn (which moves the corners of a 1x1 panel although its centre stays put) and a change of camera field.
  - `threshold_deg = REFRAME_CARRY_FRACTION x w`, where `w` is the overlap width of the **anchor's** grid: `overlap x fov_x` between columns and `overlap x fov_y` between rows, the smaller of those that exist (for a 1x1 block, `overlap x min(fov_x, fov_y)`). `REFRAME_CARRY_FRACTION = 0.5` is a named constant: the owner's first guess ("less than half the width of the overlap"), to be revisited with S7 data.
  - `max_move_deg < threshold_deg`: keep the anchor, and the counts carry. Otherwise re-anchor, and the counts restart.
  - A block with no camera field (`fov_x = fov_y = 0`) has a threshold of 0, so only an unchanged geometry keeps its anchor. With no field recorded there is no measure of "a little". Since S4 a single panel that is given its first field with nothing else changed completes its anchor instead (S4 orchestrator ruling 4; the S4 bullet below).
  - The anchor is compared with the new geometry, never with the previous save, so moves under the threshold cannot add up across saves.
  - Previews and announcements use the same function. `POST /api/framing/mosaic` takes an optional `anchor` spec and adds `reframe` to its answer, which the modal shows live (2.5). The save's answer lists every block it re-anchored (`reanchored: [{node_id, max_move_deg, threshold_deg, reason}]`, `reason` added by S4 orchestrator ruling 6), so a raw field edit in the inspector that restarts counts is announced by a toast, and CONTINUE's dropped-steps 409 (5.9) still guards the ledger.
  - The carry has a cost the constant must be judged against. A.2 already budgets half the overlap for convergence and angle error and leaves the other half for pointing error. A carried move at the limit spends that second half, so the full-depth overlap of a carried panel can shrink to nothing at the seam. The stitched union still has no hole. That is why the constant is a first guess, and S7 measures the centring residual it competes with.
  - **As built** (S3, #189). `reframe_carry` is in `catalog/framing.py`, and its answer carries one key more than the design named: `reason`, the rule that decided, asked in this order: `identity` (another object, or a name against typed coordinates), `grid`, `angle`, `unchanged` and `move`. `max_move_deg` is `None` where no move is measured, which is the first three. `unchanged`, one geometry to the identity key's precision, carries whatever the threshold, and that is what keeps the anchor of a block with no camera field, whose threshold of 0 no move is under. Each corner is placed in its own panel's tangent plane (`deproject`). A name-keyed anchor holds no coordinates, so `at=(ra_hours, dec_deg)` places it where the catalogue puts the object now. The anchor's text is `identity.anchor_for`'s, exactly the text its key hashes, so a block's first anchor keys as its S1 id did and needs no migration; `identity.anchor_geometry` reads it back (`None` for a blank one, `ValueError` for a malformed one), `identity.anchor_key` keys it, and `target_key` takes `anchor=` and the grid and keys on the anchor only when it is this block's. `POST /api/framing/mosaic` takes `anchor` as the stored text or as `MosaicAnchorIn`, answers `reframe` only for one that is not blank, and refuses malformed text with 422.
  - **As built since S4** (S4, #351, #352, #350). A first camera field completes an anchor (S4 orchestrator ruling 4). A single panel anchored with no camera field that is saved with one, its object or centre, angle and grid unchanged, keeps its anchor's keyed text and records the field beside it as `identity.RECORDED_FIELDS` (`recorded_fov_x`, `recorded_fov_y`), which `anchor_key` never reads, so its ids and counts stay (`identity.completes`, `identity.complete_anchor`, called from `save_rules._anchor_on_save`). `anchor_geometry` reads the pair back, and `reframe_carry` lays such an anchor out with the recorded field and takes its threshold from it (`framing._laid_out`), so later moves are judged against the field the camera has; the route's `_reframe_answer` completes the anchor the same way before it measures, so MATCH CAMERA's preview agrees with the save (2.5). Anything else changed in the same save, and it does not complete: a nudge made with it is judged against the anchor as it was, with no field and so a threshold of 0, because a threshold read from the field the same edit records would let that edit set its own allowance; and a grid does not complete, since its panels are tiled from the field. A verdict against an anchor whose key the current geometry keeps restarts nothing, so it is not listed and the anchor is kept, the field it records included, as on a carry (#390); only clearing the field again after a completion reaches it. Until #390 that save put the cleared geometry in the anchor's place, which forgot the recorded field, so the next save completed again with any field as though it were the block's first: a change of field that re-anchors when made in one save carried, every id kept and nothing listed, when made in two. Every `reanchored` row carries `reframe_carry`'s `reason` (S4 orchestrator ruling 6): `identity`, `grid`, `angle` or `move`, and `tests/fixtures/save_answer_rows.json` holds rows that a server test grades against the save and the UI's `flowsSaveAnswer.test.ts` reads. A save that cannot read the file it replaces refuses (#350): `FlowStore._stored` answers "nothing was keyed" only for a missing file, a file that reads but does not parse or validate, and a record with another id, and raises `StoredFlowUnreadable` for any other read error, which `_persist_flow` answers with 409 `stored_unreadable`, naming the error's type and never its text, the file's full path; a file a newer build wrote refuses there too (`NewerSchemaFlow`). A file nested past the JSON parser's depth no longer makes every save to its id a 500 (#363). Until S5 `_persist_flow`'s own read of the prior record turned a read error into no prior record, which reset its `created_ts`, `last_run` and `last_result` (#364).
  - **As built since S5** (S5, #364, #387). The store owns the record's bookkeeping. `save_and_report` takes `created_ts`, `last_run` and `last_result` (`BOOKKEEPING`) from the file it replaces, which `_stored` reads once for the anchor too (`_bookkeeping`), so a read error refuses the save as #350's does, and a missing file, a row that does not read and a record with another id give a new flow's values; `_persist_flow` makes no read of its own and only clears `readonly`. `identity.typed_coordinates` trims each field before it asks whether it was typed, so an RA or a Dec of only spaces is blank and the block is placed by its name, as the modal's mirror places it (#387); both are graded against `tests/fixtures/typed_coordinates_cases.json`. Python's `strip()` and JavaScript's `trim()` still disagree on a few characters, among them U+FEFF and U+0085 (noted on #387).
  - **As built since S7** (S7, #387, S7 orchestrator ruling 6). A coordinate is typed when it is text with something in it once trimmed, or a finite number, 0 included, since RA 0h and Dec 0 are real coordinates; null, a bool, NaN, an infinity, an integer past a float's range and anything else are blank (`identity._typed`, over `finite_number`). Until S7 a falsy value was blank and any other was its text, so an RA of the number 0 was placed by its name and `true` was typed text that did not parse and dropped the block, while the modal read 0 as "0" and `true` as "true", placing one block two ways. The modal's mirror trims as Python does (`pyStrip` in `framingModel.ts`), which closes the U+FEFF and U+0085 disagreement, and both sides are graded on `tests/fixtures/typed_coordinates_cases.json`, whose non-text cases are JSON values. `compile._text` reads every text param the same way, so a compiled entry is typed exactly when its node is: a filter, a method or a window that is `true`, NaN or a list compiles to "" where it compiled to "True", "nan" or "[1, 2]", and 0 to "0".
- Computed thresholds (from `compute_mosaic`, 25% overlap unless stated; Appendix A.5 has the method): a 3x2 of 2.0 x 1.33 deg panels allows 10.0' (a 3.4 deg turn, or a 6.0% change of field, reaches it); a single row of three such panels allows 15.0'; a 3x3 at 15% overlap allows 6.0' (1.75 deg of turn); a 2x2 of 0.9 x 0.6 deg allows 4.5'.
- **A TARGET with a name and no typed coordinates is keyed on the canonical identity the catalogue resolves its name to** (S1 hardening, #189 A5; H2, #229). `identity.target_key` is the one place this is decided; `to_plan` mints the id through it and the progress route finds the id again through it. Typed coordinates (an RA and a Dec both present, `identity.typed_coordinates`) key on the geometry as above. A name alone is resolved by `tonight.resolve_target`, the one resolver `to_plan` and the progress route both ask, which answers the coordinates, the canonical identity and whether the row moves: the catalogue id for a fixed row (a deep-sky object, a star, a typed position) and the canonical body name for a time-dependent one (a planet, the Moon, a comet, a satellite). The caller passes that identity to `target_key`, so `identity` stays pure, and it keys on `name_key`: `"name:"` followed by the first 16 hex characters of sha256 over `canonical_name`, the canonical JSON of the canonical identity with the angle and the grid spelled as in the geometry key. Such a TARGET's coordinates are the catalogue's answer at the compile's `when`, and for a planet, the Moon or a comet that answer moves by the hour, so keyed on the geometry every compile named new steps and night two banked on nothing night one shot. **A rename keeps the ids when the new name resolves to the same row**: "M 31", "M31", "m31" and "Andromeda" are all `M31`, and "jupiter" is `Jupiter`. A rename to a name that resolves to another row re-keys, and its counts restart, as a move does. Keyed on the name as typed, which A5 first did, a spelling edit restarted a deep-sky campaign. The prefix keeps a name key from ever equalling a geometry key. S1 has no grid on a TARGET, so both keys take the single-target shape; S3 passes the block's grid to the same two keys.
- `group_id = uuid5(NS_FLOWS, f"{flow_id}/{node_id}/{key}").hex`, where `key` is `geometry_key`, or the name key of the bullet above.
- `target_id = uuid5(NS_FLOWS, f"{group_id}/r{row}c{col}").hex`. A 1x1 block uses r0c0, so single targets gain continuity too.
- Pool members: `uuid5(NS_FLOWS, f"{flow_id}/{node_id}/member/{name}").hex`, keyed on the name and never the rank. A second copy of one name in the members box is keyed `{name}#1` (`identity.member_key`), so adding it later re-keys nothing.
- `step_id = uuid5(NS_FLOWS, f"{target_id}/{stage_node_id}/{signature}/{n}").hex`, where `signature` is `identity.step_signature`, `{frame_type}/{filter}/{exposure}/{gain}/{binning}` with no filter spelled empty, and `n` is the occurrence index among identical signatures inside that stage (almost always 0). Each number is spelled to `STEP_PLACES` = 6 decimals with trailing zeros and a bare trailing point trimmed: the exposure is keyed to the microsecond, and gain and binning share the six places, written as integers when integral and to at most 6 decimals otherwise. A 1234.5678 s L sub at gain 100 and bin 1 has the signature `Light/L/1234.5678/100/1`, and gain 100.5 is spelled `100.5`.
  - Why the microsecond (S1 hardening, #189 A6; H2, #189): S1 first spelled the numbers with `{:g}`, which keeps six significant digits, so every exposure of 1000 s or more lost its milliseconds, and 1234.567 s and 1234.568 s shared one count, the #77 fault. The hardening round spelled them to the millisecond, which folded the other end of the range: a planetary sub of 0.5 ms and one of 1 ms were both `0.001`, one count, and 0.1 ms was `0`. Six places keep 0.1 ms, 0.5 ms and 1 ms three recipes. No S1 or H1 id was ever deployed, so neither change needed a migration.
  - `count` is not in the id, so raising `cycles` keeps progress.
  - An exposure, gain or binning change starts a new count. That is the #77 rule: two geometries must not share a count.
  - The stage node id keeps identical steps from two stages on one panel apart.
- Without `flow_id` (an unsaved preview, or a graph-less caller), ids fall back to uuid4, as today.
- Because step ids are unique per panel, the ledger's step-id-only counting (`session.py:110-121`) stays correct with **no session schema change** (`SESSION_SCHEMA = 1`, `session.py:26`).

**Rig facts**, injected by the route the way `cool_to` is (`to_plan.py:1034-1051`):

- the live effective optics (`hub.effective_optics`, `hub.py:1987`) for M5
- the measured hop cost for M10 and the brief
- rotator presence for M8

**As built** (S3, #189). One reading of the rig, `_rig_facts` in `api/app.py`, builds a `RigFacts` (`flows/rig.py`): the camera's field at bin 1 from `hub.effective_optics`, None while the optics are unknown and never a zero field, with `fov_from` naming the profile or the rig's optics and the day it was read; the measured hop and how many hops it is the mean of, from `engine.measured_cost("hop")`, None until a hop has been timed and never the engine's seed; the rotator (`_profile_has_rotator`: True for a connected rotator or a rotator row in the active profile, False only for a profile on the native backend that lists its devices with no rotator among them, None otherwise); and whether both reject guards resolve off (`resolve_policy`). `_compile_payload` hands one object to `to_sequence_plan` and to the doctor, so M5's loss and its warning are two sentences about one field, and `run_flow`, the progress route and Tonight pass it too, so every `to_sequence_plan` call in the file is the run's. The wizard route hands it to `generate_answer` (1.8), and Tonight passes its hop as `hop_cost_s`, with the progress answer as `progress` (S3 item 5). **Since H4 the rig facts carry the guider** (H4, #506). `RigFacts` gains `guide_provider`, `guide_settle`, `guide_dither_px` and `guide_dither_every`, each validated, and `_rig_facts` fills them: the guider `providers.resolve("guide")` answers, with that guider's own settle (`_guider_and_settle`: the native engine's `NATIVE_GUIDE_SETTLE`, 1.5 px for 10 s, a copy of the Rust constants held to them by a test; PHD2's 1.5 px for 8 s; none for NINA; and no guider at all while the resolver's only answer is the bridge, its last resort, on a rig with no guider wired and no pin to the bridge), the configured dither distance and the plan's dither cadence. Tonight's brief says the GUIDE stage's sentence from these alone, never from the card's own params (`_guide_clause`; section 8's S3 item 5; `test_h4_brief_guides_from_rig.py`).

The compile never re-tiles from live optics. The node snapshot is what the engine slews to.

**`plan.count_mode = "accepted"`** when any block asks for accepted subs. A disagreement is M7. After ruling 2 a disagreement can only come from a graph written by hand or through the API, because every save rewrites `counts` to accepted subs.

### 3.4 Engine models (`server/astrodeck/sequence/models.py`)

Everything is additive, the defaults reproduce today's behaviour byte for byte, and `ui/src/types.ts` mirrors it all.

```python
class TargetGroup(BaseModel):
    id: str                                  # equals every member's mosaic_group
    name: str = ""
    kind: Literal["mosaic"] = "mosaic"
    mode: Literal["rotate", "sequential"] = "rotate"
    visit_passes: int = Field(1, ge=1, le=20)
    visit_min_s: float = Field(0.0, ge=0, le=10800)
    order: Literal["least_complete", "setting_first", "grid"] = "least_complete"
    require_centred: bool = True
    max_failed_visits: int = Field(3, ge=1, le=20)
    pa_deg: float | None = None              # layout angle, CROTA2 convention (#145)
    rotate: bool = False                     # members carry rotation_deg = pa_deg
    angle_tolerance_deg: float | None = None # None disables the angle check
    skipped_ids: list[str] = []              # target ids of skipped panels (5.9)
    geometry: dict = {}                      # provenance only: rows, cols, overlap, fov, key

class VisitBound:                            # sequence/group_rules.py; engine-internal, never persisted
    passes: int | None                       # rounds per visit (group members)
    min_s: float = 0.0                       # at least this long, checked at round boundaries
    deadline_ts: float | None = None         # end at the first frame boundary that
                                             # cannot fit the next frame: the panel's
                                             # flip point (5.7) or group_ready_ts (1.6)

# Session gains (additive, default empty, SESSION_SCHEMA stays 1):
set_aside: list[dict] = []                   # {target_id, step_id | None, reason, night}
locked_angles: dict[str, dict] = {}          # {target_id: {pa_deg, solved_at, exposed_at, source}}
group_pier: dict[str, Any] = {}              # {group_id: {night, flipped, side, verified}}

# Target gains:
center_tolerance_arcmin: float | None = Field(None, gt=0, le=30)
center_attempts: int | None = Field(None, ge=1, le=10)
autofocus_skip_if_fresh: bool = False
panel_row: int | None = None
panel_col: int | None = None
after_group: str | None = None

# SequencePlan gains:
groups: list[TargetGroup] = []
```

- An empty `groups` list gives a byte-identical run.
- A `mosaic_group` with no `groups` entry (today's Plan-UI mosaics) keeps today's panel-first behaviour.
- `angle_tolerance_deg` is computed in `to_plan` from the geometry, after convergence has taken its share (Appendix A.2).
- `Session.set_aside` makes "set aside for tonight" survive a crash: a crash-resume on the same night reads the records whose `night` matches the durable log's night key and does not retry those panels. A later night ignores them. As built (S2, #208): a record is written only through `Session.note_set_aside`, with the `events.night_key()` of the moment, the key the durable log is named by, and saved at once (`_persist_set_aside`); one is made for a panel set aside whole, for a target that sank below its own floor, and for a step its reject guard set aside (`step_id`); and `start` reads tonight's back (`Session.set_aside_on`), so a restart tonight skips them, and skips a target whose every owed step is set aside. `Session` has no `extra="forbid"` (`session.py:70`), so a build that predates the field loads the file and ignores the field. It then retries set-aside panels, which is today's behaviour. Since H4 (H4, #534, H4 orchestrator ruling 2) a record also carries `kind` and `ts`, what set the target aside and when, and `expired`: `note_set_aside_expired` marks a centring set-aside expired and saves it at once, `set_aside_on` leaves an expired record out, and `set_aside_expiries_on` counts tonight's expiries per target, so a restart tonight takes up a panel whose set-aside has expired and never expires it again, and waits for the expiry of one whose set-aside has not (5.1, 5.9). Only a set-aside for the night is still never retried that night.
- `Session.locked_angles` holds Revision 2 ruling 9's lock per target id (S2, #189), written only through `Session.lock_angle`: the first lock wins, a later call returns the lock in force, and an angle that is not a finite number is refused, because the API renders JSON without NaN and every route that returns the session would fail. Like `set_aside` it is additive, with `SESSION_SCHEMA` still 1.
- `Session.group_pier` holds each group's pier state for the night (S3, #312, S3 orchestrator ruling 4), written only through `Session.note_group_pier` and read through `Session.group_pier_on`; 5.7 says what it records and who reads it. It is typed `Any` beneath the group id, so a hand-edited value that is not a dict reads as no record instead of failing the whole file's validation, which would hide the session, ledger and all. It is additive, with `SESSION_SCHEMA` still 1, and `ui/src/types.ts` does not mirror it yet.
- `SequencePlan.skipped_ids: list[str] = []` (S4, #335) names the panels of every block the compile left out whole because each of its panels is skipped, which has no group to list them in `TargetGroup.skipped_ids`. It is absent from the dump while it is empty (a wrap serializer, `_omit_an_empty_skip`), so every plan without such a block dumps byte for byte as before, stored sessions and the plan goldens included. Nothing in the engine reads it, because nothing in the plan is shot for it; it is read through `continuation.plan_skipped_ids`, with the groups' lists, never alone (5.9). `ui/src/types.ts` does not mirror it; its `SequencePlan` is a partial mirror.

### 3.5 Identity checks at the start paths (`plan_identity_errors`)

`plan_identity_errors(plan) -> list[str]` is called on **all five** start paths:

- `/api/sequence/start`
- `/api/flows/{id}/run`, including continue
- `/api/sessions/{id}/resume` (`app.py:5350`)
- `/api/sequence/recover`
- ResumeArm

It refuses:

- duplicate target ids
- duplicate step ids
- a repeated group id (S3, #307): members, `after_group` gates, the group runs and the set-aside records all find a group by its id alone
- a group with no members
- a member listed in its own group's `skipped_ids` (S3, #307): a skipped panel is not shot and a member is, so the engine and CONTINUE would disagree about it; another group's list says nothing about it
- a target listed in the plan's own `skipped_ids` (S4, #335): that list names the panels of blocks the compile left out whole (3.4), so a target the plan shoots is in it only by the same disagreement one level up
- a calibration target in a group
- an `after_group` that names no group
- a member whose `after_group` is its own group (S3, #307): it waits while its group has live members, and it is one of them
